# JobPortal — Progress Log

> **Purpose**: Running record of what has actually shipped to `develop`. Read after `CLAUDE.md` at the start of every new session so you have current context without re-deriving it from `git log`.
>
> **What this file IS**: cumulative shipped work, currently-in-flight branches, queued follow-ups, deliberate deferrals.
>
> **What this file is NOT**: the SRS (`docs/SRS.pdf` is the source of truth for *what* to build), the project rules (`CLAUDE.md` is the source of truth for *how*), or a TODO list for new work.
>
> **Update protocol**: after a feature/bugfix/chore PR is merged to `develop`, append a new entry under "PR log" and tick the relevant box in "Phase 1 build order" if applicable. Keep the snapshot at the top fresh.

---

## Snapshot — 2026-08-08

- **Newest merge (2026-08-08)**: `feature/public-catalogs-api` — **the three reference catalogs, and the profile screen unblocked.** `GET /v1/{skills,cities,industries}` with an `?ids=` resolve mode, because `GET /me/profile` returns bare ids with no names and the app cannot render that screen without turning them into labels. Adversarial review **67/67 agents, 21 raised → 1 confirmed HIGH**, whose fix hint turned one instance into **five**: every `id` column is a Prisma `Int`, and a bigger value makes Prisma **throw** rather than match nothing — so an anonymous caller produced 500s and Sentry noise by adding a digit to a URL, including on the already-merged public `/v1/jobs/:slug`. Fixed in `@jobportal/domain`, so the **website** is repaired too. **API 899 → 919, domain 78 → 81 tests.** Full detail in the PR log below.

- **Newest merge (2026-08-08)**: `feature/public-jobs-api` — **the app can browse jobs.** `GET /v1/jobs`, `GET /v1/jobs/:slug` and the bulk `POST /v1/me/job-state`, all reusing the website's own `searchJobs` + `@jobportal/domain` rules so the two surfaces cannot disagree. Visibility is checked BEFORE the canonical 308 (the redirect's `Location` carries the title-bearing slug), and all three not-found paths are byte-identical. Adversarial review **86/86 agents, 27 raised → 5 confirmed**, all one root defect: an unhandled Elasticsearch error **answered with ES's own status, echoed its raw exception text to anonymous callers, and was skipped by Sentry as an expected 4xx** — the second facet living in the error envelope from the previous PR. Now a clean 503, a derived page bound, and an `expose`-gated http-error check; **verified by actually stopping the Elasticsearch container**. **API 858 → 899 tests.** Full detail in the PR log below.

- **Newest merge (2026-08-08)**: `feature/domain-package-extraction` — **the shared-code prerequisite the mobile spec had no phase for.** Six of its nine endpoints need rules that lived in `apps/web/lib`, which `apps/api` structurally cannot import — so six modules (slug parsing, job visibility, the SRP / directory / article param codecs, and the home aggregate) moved into a new **`@jobportal/domain`** package, with their tests. **Behaviour-preserving, and the invariant proves it: web 216 → 138 + domain 78 = exactly 216.** Two things worth remembering: React's `cache()` could not come along (RSC-only, inert in Nest), so the package exports the *uncached* home loader while `apps/web` keeps a `cache()` wrapper; and subpath imports **typechecked but failed at runtime** until an explicit `exports` map was added — a green typecheck does not prove a new package resolves. Adversarial review **65/65 agents, 20 raised → 1 confirmed**. **On pull run `pnpm install`.** Full detail in the PR log below.

- **Newest merge (2026-08-08)**: `feature/api-contract-and-mobile-auth` — **the mobile app can finally log in.** The CQ mobile team's spec asked for nine public endpoints and never mentioned the one thing blocking all of them: `JwtAuthGuard` **already accepted `Authorization: Bearer`**, so the entire authenticated API worked for a phone the instant it held a token — but nothing ever handed it one. Ships URI versioning (`VERSION_NEUTRAL`, so **every existing route keeps its path** and only opted-in controllers get `/v1`), one documented error envelope (Nest's own shape, so no existing web caller changes), and `/v1/auth/mobile/{register,login,refresh,logout}` returning tokens in the body — a **deliberate owner-approved divergence from CLAUDE.md §9**, recorded in ADR 0002. **Adversarial review run TWICE** because the first had 7 dead agents including the whole route-drift lens: **51 raised → 9 confirmed (5 distinct), all fixed**, headed by a HIGH where the rewrite left a pre-existing test red, and a MED where the new `Retry-After` **overwrote the throttler's accurate countdown with a flat 60**, making mobile back-off worse than develop. **API 812 → 858 tests.** `apps/api` only — no schema, no migration, no new flag key, no lock. Full detail in the PR log below.

## Snapshot — 2026-08-03

- **Newest merge (2026-08-03)**: `feature/sadmin-candidate-management` — **the Super Admin portal can finally see its job seekers: a Candidate Management master list, and a new rail item directly below Employer management.** Columns are name + avatar, headline, email + phone, location, registered date and an Actions column whose View / Suspend / Delete **render but do nothing** — scoped to a later PR by the owner. **The single most important decision is that the list queries `User where role=CANDIDATE` and NOT `prisma.candidate`**: the `Candidate` profile row is provisioned lazily on the first `/profile` read, email+password registration creates only the `User`, and the Google signup path swallows a failed `Candidate` create — so driving off `Candidate` would have silently omitted real registered seekers from a list whose entire job is to be complete, with no error to notice. **No schema change, no migration, no feature flag, no lock** — flags are scoped by CLAUDE.md §4 to *paid* features, and the two structurally identical sadmin lists carry none either. The profile-picture column has no candidate-owned backing field (`User.image` is populated only by Google signup, so it is null for all 20 demo rows); the owner chose to ship the initials monogram as the norm rather than invent a migration. **Adversarial 5-lens review with 3 skeptics per finding: 11 raised → 6 confirmed (4 distinct), 5 refuted, all fixed** — headed by a **HIGH** where search results changed *silently* for screen readers, because the search island was copied from recruiter `JobsFilterBar` without the `role="status"` region that page deliberately pairs with it, and a **MED** where a repeated `?q=a&q=b` genuinely 500-ed the route. A fifth defect was caught by browser verification before the review even ran: `title` beat the button content in the accessible-name computation, so all sixty action controls were named "Not available yet". Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Superseding figures — measured on THIS integrated state (2026-08-03)**: `pnpm test` fans out to **9 workspaces** — `packages/db` and `packages/ui` each gained their first test script from two branches that could not see each other, so any "8 workspaces" claim further down this file is superseded by this line. Gate green: **typecheck 12/12 · tests 9/9 (1,447 tests) · build 5/5** — API 812 / web 216 / **sadmin 216** / recruiter 83 / feature-flags 37 / ui 27 / db 20 / observability 19 / auth 17. (sadmin was 175 before the candidate list added 41.) The 7 Postgres-backed `advanceSequence` tests in `packages/db` execute for real when a local database is reachable and skip with a reason otherwise, so the gate stays green either way. 41 flags, unchanged.
- **Prior merge (2026-08-03)**: `bugfix/seed-sequence-monotonic` — **the demo seeds could rewind a sequence below existing rows, and that is why registration was returning 500.** Both seeds insert explicit ids (`Job` 100001-100050, `User` 200001-200020) and closed with an unconditional `setval(seq, <constant from the seed's own array length>, true)` — no `GREATEST`, no `max(id)`. Every row above that constant was invisible to it, including real signups and the autoincrement super-admin, so **each re-run of `pnpm db:seed:demo:full` moved the sequence backwards below live rows** and the next inserts died on the primary key (`P2002`), which nothing catches — `auth.service.ts` pre-checks email only, so it surfaced as a bare 500 on both recruiter and candidate signup. Field state that day: `User_id_seq` 200021 vs `max(id)` 200023, and the same database's **`Job_id_seq` 100053 vs `max(id)` 100240** — the identical rewind, latent only because that id range was sparse. Both sites now call **`advanceSequence()`**, which sets `GREATEST(max(id), current value, floor)` in one statement and can only move forward; a repo-wide sweep confirms these were the **only two** sequence sites in the codebase, and no seeded table is missing one. Ships the **first vitest suite in `packages/db`** — 20 tests, 7 of them against a real local Postgres on a throwaway table (gated on a local `DATABASE_URL`, skipping cleanly without one), including one that asserts the **old** `setval` still fails so the suite cannot pass vacuously. Verified through the real `PrismaClient` against the dev database, where it **repaired the live `Job_id_seq` 100053 → 100240** and correctly left `User_id_seq` untouched. **`packages/db` only** — no schema change, no migration, no new flag key, no lock. **Nothing to run on pull**; the next demo seed repairs an already-rewound sequence by itself. Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Prior merge (2026-07-30)**: `feature/brand-nav-loader` — **every page switch now says so: the “The Advance” brand loader is live in all three portals.** A tester filed “the user is unable to understand the pages are opening or not”, and it was exactly right — measured on the dev server they use, `/jobs` took **1.9–6.8s** and `/career-advice` **7s** while the App Router held the *previous* page frozen, with **zero** `loading.tsx` and no indicator anywhere in `apps/web`. **The obvious fix was the wrong one:** a route-level `loading.tsx` has already been measured in this repo degrading `notFound()` **404→200** and an over-range `redirect()` **307→200**, which on `/job/[slug]` and `/company/[handle]` would break the SEO status codes — so this is one client-side controller on Next 16.2's own APIs with **no new dependency**, no route files, and no effect on any status code. Design came from a judge-panel workflow where **“The Advance” was every judge's #1 (51/53/49)**, pitched as a full-fidelity interactive Artifact and picked by the owner. Past **250ms** of real waiting a flat white veil rises and the mark performs the brand's own promise — the cyan arrow launches from the C's seam and docks on the Q, and for **450ms of stillness the composition is the exact static lockup**, reassembled every 1.8s at a **constant tempo**, because a queue advancing at a steady rate is the trust signal and nothing here fakes progress. **The mark is vector-traced from the shipped brand PNG**, and because the arrow's resting coordinates *are* its docked position, the global reduced-motion kill rule collapses the loop to the finished lockup by construction. **Adversarial 5-lens review: 25 raised → 23 confirmed (10 distinct), all fixed** — headed by a **HIGH** where a same-URL `router.push` (a search re-submitted unchanged, a notification linking to the open page) **stranded an input-blocking veil for 15s and the retry gesture re-armed it**, a **MED** where Back over a hash-only history entry did the same, a **MED** bfcache restore that resurrected a timerless veil, and four findings that collapsed into one decision: **the veil is `pointer-events-none` for its whole life** — pure feedback, zero input interference. **packages/ui gains its first vitest harness (27 tests) — `pnpm test` now runs 8 workspaces.** Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Prior merge (2026-07-30)**: `bugfix/forgot-password-otp` — **RPT-008: password reset reworked from an emailed LINK to a 6-digit OTP, and `/forgot-password` rebuilt as “The Reset Ledger”**. The tester’s two complaints had two different causes. **No email ever arrived because `RESEND_API_KEY` is blank** — `ResendClient` logs a stub and returns, silently dropping *every* transactional email, not just the reset; that is configuration, not code, and the owner chose to defer the key and take a **strictly dev-gated** fallback (non-production AND no key) that logs the code so testers are unblocked. ⚠️ **A real Resend key is still required before launch.** The second complaint was a genuine design gap: the flow emailed a link. It now runs **email → 6-digit code → verify → new password → auto sign-in**. Schema (lock taken and released; **not purely additive** — it relaxes a NOT NULL, swaps an index for a UNIQUE, and deletes every existing row, so outstanding links stop working by design). Security is the point of the change: the attempt cap is claimed by a conditional UPDATE *before* the comparison so it fails closed under concurrency, codes are hashed at rest salted with `userId`, and unknown addresses, OAuth-only accounts, cooldowns and spent budgets all return the same shape and never throw so nothing becomes an account-existence oracle. UI chosen by the owner from a judge-panel workflow shown as a full-fidelity Artifact. **The adversarial review’s first run reported 0 findings but all four agents had died on API 529s — an infrastructure failure, not a clean result; re-run properly it raised 28 and confirmed 21 (13 distinct), all fixed** — headed by a **HIGH** where a soft-deactivated recruiter could regain a session (the eligibility check lived in `login()`, but `issueSession` is shared by login, Google OAuth and now the reset; moving it into `issueSession` also closed the Google path that predates this branch), and a **MED** where `resetWithTicket` did not fail closed because Prisma’s array-form `$transaction` commits every statement unless one rejects. **API 812 tests.** Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Prior merge (2026-07-29)**: `feature/recruiter-auth-ui-revamp` — **the recruiter portal's sign-in and sign-up pages are a two-pane split: a brand-navy panel on the left carrying a flat brand illustration, the form on the right** (SRS §4.9). Owner ask: the pages "look basic", with illustrations on the left and the form on the right, and **UI only — no functionality disturbed**. The panel is the *same fixed navy as the authed sidebar rail*, so signing in previews the shell you land in; two consequences of that surface are load-bearing rather than cosmetic — the logo must pass `onDark` (this app mounts no `ThemeProvider`, so the auto path would have put the navy logo on navy) and the focus ring must be white, because the inherited `--color-ring` measures **1.96:1** on navy, under the WCAG 1.4.11 floor (**15.37:1** after). **The illustrations are flat geometry drawn from the product's own primitives** — a job card, an applicant list with one row shortlisted, a compose card with Publish beside Save-as-draft — not stock art and not a mascot, per CLAUDE.md §2 and the `ArticleCover` precedent; the panel headline is a `<p>`, not a heading, so every page keeps exactly one `<h1>` with no h2-before-h1 in DOM order. **The shell is owned by each page rather than the `(auth)` layout, which is deleted.** The layout version resolved its copy from the request pathname and looked tidier, but it was broken: the App Router does not re-render a shared layout when you navigate between two of its own children, and the `/login` ↔ `/register` links are exactly that navigation — so clicking "Create one" left "Welcome back to your hiring desk." and the sign-in illustration beside the sign-up form until a hard reload. Moving the shell into the pages (with the two forms extracted verbatim into client islands) fixes it, keeps the panel out of the client bundle, and restores `/login` and `/register` to **`○ Static`**, matching develop's route table. **Zero functional change** — same endpoints, bodies, `credentials`, `safeNext`/`?next=`, redirects, field names, `autoComplete`, `maxLength` and `role="alert"` live region. Accessibility improved along the way: helper text is now bound with `aria-describedby`, ids come from `useId()`, `fg-subtle` hints moved to `fg-muted`, and the error text came off raw `--color-danger` (4.0:1, a pre-existing AA failure) onto the repo's theme-aware `color-mix` recipe (**6.43:1**). Adversarial 5-lens review with three independent skeptics per finding: **34 raised → 5 confirmed across 3 distinct defects, 29 refuted** — the stale panel above (raised independently by two lenses), a `headers()` call that forced all four routes dynamic behind a comment that was wrong on both of its claims, a dashed "reach" curve that ran straight through the Save-as-draft pill (11 of 21 sampled points inside it), and the decorative aside forcing page scroll on a real 1366×768 laptop (**24px over** at a ~650px viewport; the vertical rhythm now clamps against viewport height, measured **0 overflow at 650px and 600px**). **`apps/recruiter` only** — `apps/api`, `apps/web`, `apps/services`, `apps/sadmin` and every `packages/*` verified byte-untouched by an empty `git diff`; no schema, no migration, no new flag key, no shared-surface lock. **Nothing to run on pull.** +21 recruiter tests (**32 → 53**). Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Prior merge (2026-07-29)**: `feature/sadmin-employer-management` — **the Super Admin portal gains its third menu item, Employer Management**: `/employers` (the master list) and `/employers/[id]` (the employer profile). **One row per `Company`, not per recruiter** — the owner's call, and the schema agrees: several recruiters share one company, so the row key is the employer and the person beside it is *derived*. That derivation is the whole feature. **`resolveContact()` picks the active OWNER, falls back to the longest-standing active recruiter of a lesser role, and always renders the role next to the name** so a Member standing in for a removed owner can never read as the owner; a deactivated recruiter is never offered, because deactivation revokes sessions and blocks login, so they cannot be contacted at all. Precedence is an explicit `Record<RecruiterRole, number>` rather than `orderBy: { companyRole: 'asc' }`, which only yields OWNER first by accident of the enum's declaration order. **Account status carries both axes the owner asked for**: Active / Deactivated / **No account holder** primary, verification secondary — and the third state is not padding. **Four of fourteen companies on a seeded database have zero recruiters**, so contact, email and phone are simultaneously absent; rendering three em-dashes would have read as an ordinary row, when the truth is an employer with **live jobs and nobody managing them** — which the detail page now says in words. Registration date is **`Company.createdAt`**, never the owner's `Recruiter.createdAt`: self-registration into an existing company is rejected by the API, so every later recruiter joined an already-registered employer and their date means "this person joined". Verification reads `kyc?.status ?? 'NOT_SUBMITTED'` because **13 of 14 companies have no `CompanyKyc` row at all** and the schema states the row's absence *is* that state; GSTIN/PAN are deliberately absent (DPDP, and `admin-kyc` masks them even in its own list). Job counts come from a `groupBy` with **every bucket named**, so no total can fold in DRAFT — the 53-vs-52 bug this repo shipped once. **Reads Postgres directly in the RSC, with no new API module** — a deliberate reversal of this branch's own opening claim, because `lib/admin-api.ts` states the API path exists for logic that must agree with a *mutation* endpoint, and this surface is display-only. **`apps/sadmin` only** — `apps/api`, `apps/web`, `apps/recruiter`, `apps/services` and every `packages/*` verified byte-untouched by an empty `git diff`; no schema change, no migration, no new flag key, no shared-surface lock (`Building2` was already in the icon barrel). Adversarial 5-lens review with three independent skeptics per finding: **30 raised → 5 confirmed, 25 refuted** — headed by an id above int32 answering **500 instead of 404** (this is the first sadmin detail route reading Prisma directly, so `adminApiGet`'s error handling was not there to absorb the throw) and the **Deactivated / Removed labels failing WCAG AA** at 4.41:1 on the card and 4.02:1 on row hover. A `loading.tsx` was also written, **measured to silently degrade `notFound()` 404 → 200 and the over-range `redirect()` 307 → 200**, and removed. +45 sadmin tests (**86 → 131**). Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Prior merge (2026-07-29)**: `feature/navbar-mega-menu-console` — **the seeker navbar mega-menu was redesigned as "The Console"** after the owner rejected the shipped "Native tiles" look (and, before it, two editorial-column attempts). A judge-panel design Workflow generated four fresh silhouettes, three judges ranked them, and a design lead shortlisted three non-overlapping directions — Console / Bento / Marquee — pitched as **one interactive full-fidelity Artifact** on the live tokens and the real seed data; the owner picked **The Console**. It is a two-pane master/detail popover: a 216px facet rail (a real ARIA `tablist`) beside a detail pane, stitched by **ONE 3px cyan indicator that glides** between facets, over a footer with the live count, the honest quick-views and the single filled-navy CTA. Three craft moves carry it: the detail holds a fixed `min-h-[15rem]` so **the popover never resizes on swap** (measured: 298px constant across all four facets), each Jobs row carries an **honest proportion bar** (share of that facet's own max — never animated, since animating a data length would misrepresent it), and the cyan budget stays marks-only. A new `FacetTabs` client island takes the rail icons, every pane and the footer as **server-rendered ReactNode slots**, so no presentational code enters the client bundle and every link stays crawlable; inactive panes use `hidden` so they leave the tab order and the a11y tree. Adversarial 4-lens review: **19 raised → 13 confirmed (8 distinct), all fixed**, headed by two real interaction bugs — clicking a facet focused the button and defeated the mouse-leave auto-close (pinning an 834px panel to the sticky header), and a stray pointer over the rail destroyed keyboard focus inside the open pane and closed the whole popover. **`apps/web` only** — no schema/migration/flag; `theme.css` and the icon barrel untouched. +2 web tests (web **214 → 216**). Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Prior merge (2026-07-29)**: `feature/job-moderation-queue-ui` — **job moderation is now ON, and the queue that makes it safe exists.** PR 2 of 3. `apps/sadmin` gains **`/jobs`** (the review queue) and **`/jobs/[id]`** (the posting, with Approve / Send back). The console offers **two views rather than a raw status filter** — filtering on `JobStatus` was the obvious design and it lies, because `ACTIVE` is every live job on the platform and almost none of it was ever moderated, so an "approved" tab would have listed 52 jobs nobody reviewed; `pending` is what awaits a decision and `decided` is `reviewedAt IS NOT NULL`, which is also **the only place a moderation decision is readable at all** (decisions write `ProfileAuditLog` rows and that table has no reader anywhere). This is the app's first **mutation** surface, so it adds `lib/admin-api.ts` — the server-side reader for AdminGuard'd endpoints, forwarding the HttpOnly cookie; the dashboard still reads Postgres directly, while the queue goes through the API because the list and detail already exist as tested service methods and re-querying here would fork logic that must agree with what the decision endpoint enforces. **Flipping the flag took two mechanisms, not one**: `seedFlags` upserts with `update: {}` so a new seed default only ever reaches a database seeded from scratch, and the companion migration `enable_job_moderation_flag` moves the ones that already have the row. Recruiter-facing: the badge reads **"Under review"**, publishing now confirms what actually happened (it used to redirect silently, which reads as failure when the job lands in review), Jobs-by-status always shows the review row, and the edit page no longer says "changes to a live job go public immediately" beneath an "Under review" badge. Adversarial 5-lens review: **35 raised → 10 confirmed (7 distinct), all fixed** — headed by a Reopen dialog still promising the job "goes live again immediately", and a salary formatter that printed a **ceiling as a floor** (`₹12+ LPA` for a job capped at ₹12 LPA) with a unit test that had asserted the wrong string. Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Prior merge (2026-07-29)**: `feature/job-moderation-api` — **the `moderation.jobs.enabled` trapdoor is closed.** That flag routes newly published jobs to `PENDING_MODERATION`, but that status had **no exit anywhere in the codebase** — `close()` and `reopen()` rejected it, `publish()` only accepted DRAFT, PATCH never wrote status, and the nightly expiry sweep selects ACTIVE only — so turning the flag on stranded every job posted thereafter, with `DELETE` as the sole recovery. This PR (**1 of 3**) ships the backend: a new **`admin-jobs`** module (`GET /admin/jobs`, `GET /admin/jobs/:id`, `PATCH /admin/jobs/:id`) behind `AdminGuard`, where the decision is an **atomic status-guarded `updateMany` committed in one transaction with its `ProfileAuditLog` row**, so two admins clicking Approve cannot both index the job and both notify. **Rejection returns the job to DRAFT with a reason** rather than adding a `JobStatus.REJECTED` — which keeps the five hand-maintained copies of the status set untouched and lets the recruiter fix and resubmit through the existing `publish()` path. Approval is not a status write: **`JobPublishEffectsService` was extracted** so it replays the exact ES-index + alert-fanout + cache-purge + email sequence a recruiter publish fires, and re-clears a stale `expiresAt` — a job can sit in review past its own expiry where the ACTIVE-only sweep cannot see it, and would otherwise go live and be expired again within 24h with the recruiter's quota already spent. Also fixes **two real holes this work exposed**: `reopen()` set ACTIVE unconditionally without ever reading the flag (Close → Reopen was a two-click bypass, and since PATCH may rewrite a CLOSED job's text, Close → Edit → Reopen could publish content no admin had seen), and the **public `/job/[slug]` page served DRAFT and PENDING_MODERATION jobs to anyone with the URL** — full description, salary and a `JobPosting` JSON-LD block — with the canonical 308 leaking the title in a `Location` header before any status check ran. **The flag stays OFF in this PR**; flipping it on ships with the review UI in PR 2. Adversarial 5-lens review: **33 raised → 7 confirmed (2 distinct), all fixed**, both of them defects this branch introduced in its own `reopen()` change. Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Prior merge (2026-07-29)**: `feature/navbar-mega-menu` — the seeker navbar's **Jobs** and **Companies** links now open a hover/focus **mega-menu** (the "Native tiles" concept — the homepage browse-grid tile language: a navy `primary-100` icon chip + label + live count + cyan hover chevron), while a plain click still navigates to `/jobs` / `/companies`. 100% real data from a new 30-min-cached **`loadNavMenuData()`** (a date-free subset of `loadHomePageData`): roles/cities/industries/skills → canonical SRP filters, companies → directory collections + top-rated employers (reusing `CompanyLogo`), every link built through `buildSrpHref`/`buildDirectoryQuery` (no 301). Content is server-rendered and `inert` when closed (crawlable, but out of the tab order + AT tree); the **slot pattern** keeps the presentational panels out of the client bundle. Desktop-only (`lg+`; mobile drawer untouched); fixed panel widths → **no overflow at 1024/1360**; all text AA+. Design chosen by a judge-panel workflow ("The Index" was rejected → "Native tiles" approved via a full-fidelity Artifact). Adversarial 4-lens review: **12 raised → 11 confirmed, all fixed** (Escape-from-inside reopen guard, the documented-but-missing outside-pointerdown close, hover-close focus retention, focus-restore only when focus was inside, dropped `aria-haspopup` on the region disclosure, `sr-only` "Rating" label, `React cache()` on `loadHomePageData`). **`apps/web` only** — no schema/migration/flag/theme-token change. +11 web tests (web **188 → 199**). Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Prior merge (2026-07-29)**: `feature/sadmin-dashboard-insights` — the Super Admin dashboard gains the three sections the owner asked for: **Pending approvals** (company verification + job postings awaiting review), **New signups** (today / 7d / 30d with a 30-day daily bar chart), and **Platform activity** (jobs posted + applications received over 30 days as a two-series line chart). **Charts are hand-rolled SVG — no chart library was added**, matching the recruiter dashboard's approach and the owner's earlier call; the geometry lives in a pure, unit-tested module and every chart ships a visually-hidden data table so it is not an opaque image to a screen reader. Day buckets are computed **in IST**, via a double `AT TIME ZONE` conversion that was verified against the database — a single conversion is wrong in *both* directions on these `timestamp WITHOUT time zone` columns, and since India is UTC+5:30 the naive version would misfile the last 5h30m of every Indian day. **`apps/sadmin` only** — no schema change, no migration, no new feature-flag key (it only *reads* `moderation.jobs.enabled`, through `@jobportal/feature-flags` per CLAUDE.md §4). Adversarial 5-lens review: **27 raised → 8 confirmed (6 distinct), all fixed**, headed by a HIGH defect where "Jobs posted" counted unpublished DRAFTs behind a comment asserting the opposite — and where the unit test had baked in the same false premise, so the suite asserted the bug instead of catching it. Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Superseding figures**: **recruiter 32 → 53** (+21 auth-panel invariants — pure, because `apps/recruiter`'s vitest only runs `lib/**`); prior: sadmin 86 → 131, web 214 → 216. Gate baseline: **typecheck 12/12 · tests 7/7 · build 5/5** (API 683 / web 216 / **recruiter 53** / sadmin 131 / auth 17 / feature-flags 37 / observability 19). **No migration and no new flag key in the employer-management merge — nothing to run on pull.** **41 flags — no new key across either PR; `moderation.jobs.enabled` is now seeded ON.** Three migrations across the two PRs: `add_job_moderation`, `add_job_review_queue_index_and_quota_flag`, `enable_job_moderation_flag` — **on pull run `pnpm db:generate && pnpm db:migrate:dev`.** ⚠️ **Behaviour change for every developer**: after migrating, a published job no longer goes live immediately — it lands in **Under review** and needs approving at `/sadmin/jobs` (sign in with the seeded `admin@careerqueue.in`). Turning it back off is one toggle in `/admin/feature-flags`, and existing pending jobs stay actionable either way.
- **Prior figures (PR 1, `feature/job-moderation-api`)**: API 637 → 680 (+43: admin-jobs service 19 + admin-jobs dto 17 + reopen-moderation 4 + quota refund 3) and web 199 → 214 (+15 job-visibility).

---

## Snapshot — 2026-07-28

- **Newest merge (2026-07-28)**: `feature/sadmin-portal` — **a third portal joins the product: `apps/sadmin`, the internal Super Admin console** (port 3003, served at the `/sadmin` slug via `basePath`). This is the staff-facing side of the platform — the surface where job posts will be reviewed and approved, recruiter business verification (KYC) processed, and support tickets handled. This PR lays the foundation: the **sign-in page** (email + password, no signup, with a show/hide eye toggle) and the first menu item, **Dashboard**, showing three platform totals — active recruiters, registered job seekers, and OPEN jobs. **No schema change, no migration, no new feature-flag key, no shared-surface lock**; `packages/ui`, `theme.css`, `packages/types`, `keys.ts`, `apps/web`, `apps/recruiter` and `apps/services` are all **byte-untouched**. It reuses the existing **`UserRole.ADMIN`** rather than adding a SUPER_ADMIN tier, and ships a **seeded `admin@careerqueue.in` account** so every developer can sign in immediately after `pnpm db:seed`. A notable side effect: that account is the **first ADMIN user this repo has ever had**, so it also unlocks the pre-existing `/admin` console inside `apps/web` (feature flags, KYC review, support), which until now literally nobody could log into. Adversarial 5-lens review: **31 raised → 20 confirmed (11 distinct), all fixed**, headed by two HIGH defects in the seed that would each have shipped broken. Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Superseding figures**: the gate baseline moves to **typecheck 12/12 · tests 7/7 · build 5/5** (was 11/11 · 6/6 · 4/4) — `pnpm build` now means **five** apps. Test counts: **API 637** (+8) / web 188 / recruiter 32 / **sadmin 17 (new workspace)** / auth 17 / feature-flags 37 / observability 19. 41 flags (unchanged — admin surfaces are deliberately not flag-gated).

## Snapshot — 2026-07-26

- **Newest merge (2026-07-26)**: `feature/recruiter-brand-theme` — the **recruiter portal now wears the job-seeker dashboard's colour system**, so the two portals read as one product: a solid brand-navy sidebar rail with brand-cyan accents, a muted canvas, and white elevated cards/top bar. The load-bearing discovery is that **`apps/web` and `apps/recruiter` already import the same `packages/ui/src/styles/theme.css`** — the navy/cyan tokens were already available to the recruiter app, which simply never used them (**zero `--color-accent-*` occurrences** beforehand). So the re-skin required **no change to `theme.css` or `packages/ui`**, and `apps/web` / `apps/services` / `apps/api` / `packages/*` are **byte-untouched** (verified by an empty `git diff develop`). Visual only — no route, query, handler, flag check, guard or prop contract changed. Adversarial 4-lens review: **12 raised → 6 confirmed (5 distinct), all fixed**, headed by a real regression the navy rail introduced — the inherited `:focus-visible` ring fell to **1.96:1** on navy (fixed to white, **15.37:1**) — plus three shared-component fills (`Badge` neutral, `Skeleton`, `EmptyState`) that collided with the new canvas. Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Superseding figures**: test counts **unchanged** (API 629 / web 188 / recruiter 32 / auth 17 / feature-flags 37 / observability 19), which is the expected outcome for a visual-only change. 41 flags (unchanged).

- **Prior merge (2026-07-26)**: `feature/recruiter-dashboard-insights` — the recruiter **Dashboard** is no longer an empty-state stub. **Verification progress leads the page** (work email · company profile · company KYC, the last mirroring the API's own five submit predicates so the card can't promise a submission the API would reject), followed by KPI tiles (jobs posted / open / draft · applications received · needs review · **candidates hired**), a candidate pipeline, jobs-by-status, a needs-attention list and top jobs. **Optimised for the post-sign-in landing page**: verification needs 1 query and paints with the shell while the 6 aggregates stream behind `<Suspense>` + the repo's **first `loading.tsx`** — measured 167ms shell → 261ms verification → 321ms KPIs, vs 322ms blocking. Metrics with no backing data (views/impressions, time-to-hire, saved-job interest) were **omitted, not faked**. Also **stood up the first vitest harness in `apps/recruiter`** (32 tests — `pnpm test` had been silently skipping the workspace) and fixed two pre-existing shell bugs (top-bar KYC badge ignored its killswitch; four serial awaits in the layout). Adversarial 4-lens review: **33 raised → 17 confirmed, all fixed**. **`apps/recruiter` only** — no schema, no migration, no new flag. Full detail in the PR log below.
- **Superseding figures**: **API 629 / web 188 / recruiter 32 (new workspace) / auth 17 / feature-flags 37 / observability 19** — `pnpm test` now runs **6** workspaces, not 5. 41 flags (unchanged).

- **Prior merge (2026-07-20)**: `feature/recruiter-plans-always-visible` — the recruiter **Plans & Billing** surface is now visible to **every** recruiter, with **Free** shown as the current plan. Introduces the **visibility vs purchasability** split: new **`recruiter.plans_visible`** (seeded **ON** ⇒ **41 flags**) gates L1/nav/L2, while **`subscription.system.enabled` stays OFF** and now gates purchasing only. That master flag is deliberately *not* flipped because it also feeds `apps/web`'s `ApplyButton` upgrade CTA, which links to a **non-existent `/pricing`** — flipping it would have shown rate-limited job seekers a 404 link. Paid cards render priced but with a **"Coming soon"** status instead of a CTA; RECRUITER plans ship **listed**, CANDIDATE plans stay dark. **API 629 tests** (+1). Full detail + the 8 fixed review findings are in the PR log below.
- **Superseding figures**: **41 flags** (was 40) · **API 629 / web 188 / auth 17 / feature-flags 37 / observability 19**. The per-line figures further down this snapshot predate this merge.

It reuses the existing **`killswitch.recruiter_post_job`** (L2 menu hide + L3 503) + create()'s verified-email gate, validates the stored draft's mandatory fields (400 with the gaps **before** consuming quota), consumes the post quota, honours moderation (ACTIVE vs PENDING_MODERATION), refreshes `postedAt`, clears a stale `expiresAt`, and fires the publish side-effects + email. The DRAFT→final flip is an **atomic `updateMany` guarded on `status:'DRAFT'`** (delete() pattern) so a double-click race can't double-consume/double-email (loser refunds; failure path refunds too). Sorts cross-checked vs Postgres (exact match incl. page-seam integrity). **Prior recruiter merge**: `feature/recruiter-jobs-candidate-counts` — the recruiter **Jobs list** (`/jobs`) gained **candidate-count columns**: each row shows four clickable metrics — **Responses** (all applications) · **New** (`APPLIED`) · **Shortlisted** (`SHORTLISTED`) · **Matches** (applicants whose candidate `skillIds` overlap the job's). Counts are computed server-side in the RSC (one `application.groupBy(['jobId','status'])` for Responses/New/Shortlisted + a bounded set of per-job matched `count`s) — **no API change** (reads/writes split). Each number links into the applicants list, which gained an additive **`?filter=new|shortlisted|matched`** param + an `ApplicantsFilterTabs` (All/New/Shortlisted/Matches) row, filter-aware header/empty copy, and filter-preserving pagination; **own jobs link, teammate/zero counts render as plain text** (the applicants page is owner-private). Because the 9-column table exceeded the shell's `max-w-3xl` reading cap (a real horizontal page scrollbar ≤~1279px), the Jobs page **opts into the wider content column** (`data-wide` → layout `max-w-6xl`) and `body:has([data-wide])` clips document-level horizontal overflow **only for that page** — the table still scrolls inside its own card; other recruiter pages unchanged. New `JobsTable` metric columns + `applicant-filter.ts` + `ApplicantsFilterTabs.tsx`. **Recruiter-only; no schema/migration/feature-flag/shared-surface-lock; `apps/web`+`apps/services` byte-untouched.** Adversarial review: **7 raised, 3 confirmed & fixed** (MED zero-count WCAG-AA contrast; LOW filter-tab narrowing; NIT keyboard-scrollable table region). Counts cross-checked vs Postgres (all 7 demo jobs exact); no horizontal page scroll verified at 375/1024/1280/1440. **Intervening `apps/web` seeker merges (teammates):** `feature/companies-directory-redesign`, `feature/home-hero-copy`, `feature/srp-expanding-search`. **Prior recruiter merge**: `feature/recruiter-jobs-filters` — the recruiter **Jobs list** (`/jobs`) gained a **Filters & Search bar** (SRS §4.9): debounced **search by job title OR numeric Job ID** + **Status / Category (jobType: Free·Hot Vacancy·SMB·Internship) / Location / Posted-by** dropdowns, URL/searchParams-driven with pagination preserving all params; the old status pill row is **folded into the bar** (`JobsStatusFilter` retired). To give Posted-by teammates to list, the Jobs list is **broadened from own-jobs (`postedById`) to company-wide (`companyId`)**; because the applicants page + close/reopen API stay **own-jobs-only**, teammate rows are **view-only** — a new `JobLink` helper gates the title/applicant-count links (and Close/Reopen) to the poster, so no teammate row shows a 404-bound link/button. New `JobsFilterBar`/`JobLink`/`JOB_TYPE_LABELS`. **Recruiter-only; no schema/migration/feature-flag/shared-surface-lock; `apps/web`+`apps/services` byte-untouched.** Adversarial review: **6 findings raised, all confirmed, all fixed & re-verified** (HIGH teammate-link 404; MED debounced-search stale-snapshot + mid-typing input clobber; LOW missing aria-live + undimmed disabled-select chevron). **Prior merge**: `feature/recruiter-jobs-list-table` — the recruiter **Jobs list** (`/jobs`) is now a structured **table** (Job Title · Location · Date Posted · Status, + Applicants · Actions; responsive desktop-table/mobile-cards) with **color-coded status badges** — Open (green) · Draft (grey) · Expired (amber) · Closed (red) — via a new reusable `JobStatusBadge`/`JOB_STATUS_META`, WCAG-AA (the AA foreground fix is scoped to the badge so the shared `packages/ui/Badge.tsx` stays untouched → `apps/web`/`apps/services` unaffected). Location was newly surfaced by widening the page's own RSC Prisma read (`primaryCity`/`locality`/`workMode` — no schema change); the "Active" filter pill became "Open"; and the Post-a-Job `TemplatePicker` was unified onto the same shared status map (it had drifted to "Active"/grey-Expired). Recruiter-only; **no schema/migration/flags/locks**. **Prior merge**: `feature/recruiter-post-a-job` (**Phase 4 — feature COMPLETE**) — the **Post a Job** revamp is done across four phases. **P4** (this merge): a Bold/Bullets/Heading **Markdown editor** on the Description field (no new dependency) stored to a new nullable `Job.descriptionMarkdown`; the seeker `JobBody` renders it through the **existing sanitized** `renderArticleMarkdown` pipeline when present (else the unchanged plain-text path — **zero regression**; the only `apps/web` touch, `apps/services` untouched); plus two live read-only sidebar widgets — a **Salary Trends** panel (`GET /recruiter/jobs/salary-trends`) + a **Reach Meter** (`GET /recruiter/jobs/reach`). **On pull run `pnpm db:generate && pnpm db:migrate:dev`.** **P3** (prior): the **rich Job Details form + the feature's first schema change** — `enum JobType` + `Job.jobType`/`openings`/`qualifications`/`internshipDurationMonths` + a `Locality` model (City→Area); seeds add **20 departments** (fixing a long-empty dropdown) + **84 localities**; `PostJobWizard` rebuilt into a single-column sectioned form. **Phase 2** (prior): the pre-wizard flow — a `PostJobFlow` orchestrator with an Indeed-style **Template picker** + a Naukri-style **Job-type selector** (Free/Internship live; Hot Vacancy/SMB gated OFF behind `recruiter.hot_vacancy.enabled` + `recruiter.smb_pack.enabled`). **Phase 1** (prior): a dedicated **`/post-job`** page (the 6-step `PostJobWizard` moved off `/jobs/new`, which now 307-redirects), a **"Post a Job"** sidebar item below Jobs, both "Post a job" CTAs (Jobs page + the previously-dead Dashboard button) repointed, and a new **`killswitch.recruiter_post_job`** (seeded OFF ⇒ LIVE; L2 page 404 / L3 create 503). Remaining phases: the rich Job Details form (+ `JobType`/`openings`/`qualifications`/`Locality` migration), a rich-text editor (+ one additive `apps/web/JobBody` tweak), and salary-trends/reach-meter. (Between this and billing, several `apps/web` job-seeker UX merges landed — see the PR log: `navbar-nav-and-brand`, `srp-search-header`, `signed-in-header-auth-state`, `job-search-srp-redesign`, `clickable-job-cards`, etc.) Prior monetization merge: `feature/recruiter-billing` — recruiter **Plans & Billing** (SRS §4.11 / §7): Razorpay Orders + hosted Checkout for **prepaid, company-scoped** recruiter plans (no auto-renew), GST invoices (pdfkit → private R2, streamed download), webhook capture with an idempotency ledger, `/plans` + `/billing` pages, and a sidebar **Billing** group. Gated on the pre-existing **`subscription.system.enabled`** (Pattern B, seeded OFF ⇒ hidden; three-layer L1 middleware / L2 page / L3 API) — **no new flag key**. Two additive migrations (`20260702135653_add_recruiter_billing` + `20260702150244_add_invoice_line_snapshot`): `PlanAudience` + `SubscriptionPlan.audience`, `Subscription.companyId`, GST-extended `SubscriptionInvoice`, new `PaymentOrder`/`PaymentWebhookEvent`/`CompanyBillingProfile`, 4 `BILLING_*` audit actions. **Gateway decision: Razorpay primary (Stripe is invite-only in India in 2026), inverting CLAUDE.md §1** — recorded in ARCHITECTURE.md §10. **apps/web + apps/services byte-untouched.** Prior: `feature/recruiter-user-management` — recruiter **Users / Team management**: in-company RBAC (`RecruiterRole` Owner/Admin/Member) + per-module permissions, emailed single-use invites, remove (soft-deactivate + re-login block), `/users` panel + public `/accept-invite/[token]`; `killswitch.recruiter_user_management`; registration now new-company-only. Prior: `feature/recruiter-change-password` — recruiter **Settings** sidebar group + **Change Password**. Prior: `feature/recruiter-notifications` — recruiter **Notification settings + top-bar bell**. Prior: `feature/recruiter-company-verification` — recruiter **Company Verification (KYC)**: `/kyc` tab + admin review console + `killswitch.recruiter_kyc`. (Earlier note still applies: the PR log between ~2026-05-17 and the recruiter-single-email entry is incomplete — several `develop` merges predate disciplined logging; treat older snapshot detail as approximate.)
- **Local runtime status (DB verified 2026-07-02)**: Docker (Postgres 18 + Redis 8 + Elasticsearch 9.4) up; both billing migrations applied; Prisma client regenerated; seed upserts **6 plans**; `pnpm build` green for all 4 apps. API (`:4000`) + recruiter (`:3001`) dev servers booted this session; web (`:3000`) not booted. (Note: recruiter billing had been toggled ON in the local DB via `apps/api/scripts/enable-recruiter-billing.ts` for an earlier demo. **As of 2026-07-20 it has been reset OFF** — `subscription.system.enabled` + all three `subscription.plans.*` keys are back at their seeded Day-0 values, which is the state `feature/recruiter-plans-always-visible` was verified against. `recruiter.plans_visible` is ON by seed. Note the script does not exit on its own — its Redis pub/sub handle keeps the process alive, so Ctrl-C it once the writes are logged.)
- **Seed catalogue**: **40 flags** (+`killswitch.recruiter_job_collaborate` this merge; +`killswitch.recruiter_job_delete` prior; +`recruiter.hot_vacancy.enabled` + `recruiter.smb_pack.enabled` prior; +`killswitch.recruiter_post_job` + `killswitch.recruiter_help_support` prior) / 10 industries / 50 cities / **84 localities** / **20 functional-areas (departments)** / 160 skills / **6 plans** (added Recruiter Starter ₹1,999/mo + Recruiter Growth ₹4,999/mo; enterprise-yearly reclassified to RECRUITER audience) / 3 articles. Plus the demo overlay (stable Job IDs 100001-100050): 12 companies / 8 recruiters (each their company's **Owner**) / 38 reviews / 50 jobs / 20 candidates / 371 applications.
- **Test counts on develop**: **628 API** (+13 this merge: the `recruiter-job-collaborators` service suite (12 — owner-only 404 / killswitch 503 / self-add·cross-company·deactivated 400s / happy-path row+notification / idempotency / remove) + 1 applicants "a collaborator can transition"; the prior create(PUBLISH) field-requirement DTO suite added +9 — DRAFT-lenient / PUBLISH-with-all-fields / PUBLISH-missing-each-of-city·dept·openings / message-names-only-the-gaps + the `missingPublishFields` helper's complete·empty·boundary cases; the prior `feature/recruiter-publish-draft` `publish()` suite added +10, and `feature/recruiter-draft-jobs` added 0 — both recruiter-only) + 186 web (+5 from seeker `companies` params) + 37 feature-flags + 19 observability + 17 auth = ~855 unit tests (candidate-count columns added **0** — the recruiter app has no unit-test harness, consistent with every prior recruiter PR) (recruiter-billing added 67 API tests: `recruiter-billing.service` 33 + `dto` 8 + `gst`/invoice-number 11 + `razorpay.client` 6 + `invoice-pdf` 2 + `tier-resolver` 5, plus the post-quota mock updated in place).

### PR — `feature/brand-nav-loader` · 2026-07-30 — global navigation loading feedback (“The Advance”)

**Tester report:** “Add Loading(loader) — currently the user is unable to understand the pages are
opening or not… user is unable to understand that job or companies pages are opening or not.”

**Reproduced and measured before designing anything.** On the dev server the tester uses, `/jobs`
answered in **6.8s cold / 1.9s warm**, `/career-advice` in **7.0s**, `/companies` in **3.0s** — and
during a client-side navigation the App Router holds the **previous page frozen on screen** for that
entire time. `apps/web` had **zero** `loading.tsx` files and no navigation indicator of any kind;
recruiter and sadmin had exactly one dashboard skeleton each. The complaint was precisely accurate.

**Why not `loading.tsx` everywhere — the obvious fix is the wrong one here.** This repo has already
measured a route-level fallback silently degrading `notFound()` **404 → 200** and an over-range
`redirect()` **307 → 200** (the `feature/sadmin-employer-management` review, where a written
`loading.tsx` was deleted for exactly this). On `/job/[slug]` (expired jobs 404, slug drift 308) and
`/company/[handle]` that would break the SEO contract the sitemap and canonical work depends on. So
the mechanism is one **client-side controller** built on Next 16.2's own `useLinkStatus`-era APIs
— **no new dependency**, no route files, no effect on any status code.

**Design: judge-panel workflow → Artifact → owner pick.** Four concepts (full-screen veil / top-edge
strip / floating chip / wildcard staged) were scored by three judges on brand fit, calm, semantic
honesty, uniqueness, feasibility and a11y. **“The Advance” took every judge's #1 (51 / 53 / 49)**
and was pitched beside the repaired staged runner-up as a **full-fidelity interactive Artifact** with
live 1.5s / ≈4s / 7s / reduced-motion scenarios on the real tokens. The owner picked A; B was
declined along with its prerequisite PNG→SVG logo migration across three shells and the sign-off
that rearranging the lockup would have needed.

**What ships.** Past **250ms** of genuine waiting a flat white veil rises and the mark performs the
brand's own promise: the cyan arrow launches out of the C's seam, glides right, docks on the Q — and
for **450ms of total stillness the composition is the exact static lockup**, reassembled every 1.8s.
The tempo **never changes**: a queue advancing at a constant rate is the trust signal, so a 7-second
wait is pixel-identical cycles plus one quiet “Still loading” caption at 3s. Nothing fakes progress.

**The mark is traced, not drawn.** Every coordinate comes from row/column pixel scans of the shipped
`cq-mark-color.png`: the C is a stroked arc at center (134,76) r 61.5 width 33 whose **butt cap at
42° reproduces the brand's diagonal terminal cut**, and the arrow paints *over* the C's lower stroke
exactly as the PNG composites it. The arrow's **resting coordinates are its docked position**, which
is what makes reduced motion correct for free — `theme.css`'s global kill rule collapses the loop to
the **finished lockup**, and a media query swaps the 3s caption for an immediate “Loading” so text
carries the signal when nothing moves.

**Per-portal surfaces.** `apps/web` mounts the full veil from the root layout (inside `<Suspense>`,
since the wrapper reads `useSearchParams`). Recruiter and sadmin mount a **100% opaque pane-only
veil offset `md:left-[256px]`** from their `(authed)` layouts — browser-measured left:256
width:1024 at 1280px — because a translucent sheet ghosting the fixed navy rail reads as a
compositing bug rather than a loading state.

**Timing is a framework-free machine** (`packages/ui/src/lib/nav-progress.ts`): 250ms show delay ·
400ms min-visible floor · 170ms CSS exit · 15s failsafe · re-navigation **reuses the running loop**
(a restarting arrow would read as the queue resetting). Detection is a document **bubble-phase**
click listener that **deliberately ignores `defaultPrevented`** — Next's `<Link>` preventDefaults
every client-side navigation, so “prevented” is exactly what a real nav looks like, while controls
that hijack a click inside a link (`JobCardSaveToggle`) also `stopPropagation` and never reach the
document. Each wrapper patches `router.push/replace` so SRP filters, sorts, header search and
pagination signal too; `refresh()` is **deliberately unpatched** because it never changes the route
key. **First vitest harness in `packages/ui`** → `pnpm test` now runs **8** workspaces.

**Adversarial 5-lens review with an independent refuter per finding: 25 raised → 23 confirmed
(10 distinct), all fixed.**
- **HIGH — a same-URL `router.push` stranded an input-blocking veil for 15 seconds**, and the
  natural retry gesture re-armed the failsafe for another 15s each time. Real triggers existed: a
  header or SRP search re-submitted unchanged, Enter on an empty `/career-advice` search, a
  recruiter notification linking to the page already open. Fixed with a new `isSameDocumentNav`
  guarding all three patches — canonicalized query comparison so `%20` vs `+` cannot defeat it,
  basePath-aware for `/sadmin`, and reading `window.location` **at call time** because the patch
  installs once and closed-over hook values would go stale.
- **MED — Back/Forward across a hash-only history entry** (the real `#openings` anchors on company
  pages) fired a navStart that no route-key change would ever answer. popstate now compares the
  post-traversal location against the last committed one.
- **MED — a bfcache restore resurrected a veil whose timers, failsafe included, had died with
  `pagehide`.** pagehide now clears the React state too and `pageshow(persisted)` resets again.
- **Four findings collapsed into one decision: the veil is `pointer-events-none` for its whole
  life** — pure feedback, zero input interference. That closed both the invisible ~80ms blocking
  window between the 170ms fade and the unmount, and the WCAG 2.2 inequity of a sheet that blocked
  a mouse but not a keyboard through an opaque surface.
- **LOW/NIT:** the min-visible floor moved to `performance.now()` (a backwards NTP step would hold
  the veil for the step's duration with the failsafe already cleared); the click predicate compares
  canonicalized queries; the live region now escalates to “Still loading” at 3s and announces
  “taking longer than expected” on a failsafe instead of leaving “Loading page…” as the last thing
  heard; re-navigation during the exit no longer blinks the veil through transparent; the
  clip-path's comment, which was **geometrically false**, now matches the real slant line; ring
  crowns render via `overflow-visible` rather than a flat cut wider than the brand's own tangency;
  the barrel exports only the two symbols the wrappers consume.

**Gate green on the integrated state:** typecheck **12/12** · tests **8/8** (API 812 / web 216 /
recruiter 83 / sadmin 175 / **ui 27, new** / auth 17 / feature-flags 37 / observability 19) · build
**5/5**. **Browser-verified in all three portals**: the veil shows and exits on real slow navs,
`pointer-events: none` measured on the live element, keyframes bound, `role="status"` announcing,
pane geometry measured, 0 console errors — and every fixed stranding scenario re-verified
(same-query re-submit → **no veil ever**; `#openings` click then Back → **no veil ever**; a genuine
cross-page navigation still shows it).

**Known gap, stated:** recruiter/sadmin `/login` sit outside the authed shell and have no loader.
They are static sub-250ms routes, below the show threshold, so nothing would ever paint.
**Follow-up:** a `RESEND_API_KEY` is still unprovisioned (unrelated, unchanged).


### PR — `feature/api-contract-and-mobile-auth` · 2026-08-08 — mobile API foundation (ADR 0002, steps 1–2)

**Context.** The CQ mobile team (Flutter, separate repo) sent `API_SPEC_FOR_MOBILE.md` asking for nine
public REST endpoints, framing each as *“a thin REST wrapper over the exact same query/loader the SSR
page already runs”*. Verified claim-by-claim against this codebase by 8 parallel agents, that framing
is **wrong about this repo**: `apps/web` is server-rendered and calls `@jobportal/search` and Prisma
**in-process**, so there is no HTTP layer to wrap, and `apps/api` cannot import `apps/web` at all.
Seven of the nine are net-new Nest modules. Full analysis in **`docs/adr/0002-public-rest-api-for-mobile.md`**
(local-only — `/docs/` is gitignored; the owner distributes it manually).

**The blocker the spec never mentioned.** `JwtAuthGuard` **already accepts `Authorization: Bearer`**
(`jwt-auth.guard.ts:13-16`) and every job-seeker controller sits behind it — so the whole authenticated
API works for a phone the instant it holds a token. It had no way to get one: register, login, refresh
and reset-password all return `{user}` and emit tokens **only** via `Set-Cookie`, and `/auth/refresh`
reads the refresh token **exclusively** from the cookie (`auth.controller.ts:183`). The app could
present a credential it could not acquire. That gated all nine endpoints and was not on the spec's
critical path. (The spec was also wrong in the app's favour: **applying already works** —
`POST /me/applications` is complete and `apps/web` itself uses it over plain HTTP, so mobile apply is
zero backend work.)

**What shipped.**
- **URI versioning** (`main.ts`) with `defaultVersion: VERSION_NEUTRAL`. The load-bearing choice: every
  existing controller keeps its exact current path, and only a controller opting in with
  `version: '1'` moves to `/v1`. A plain `setGlobalPrefix('v1')` would have relocated all 36
  controllers and broken `apps/web`, `apps/recruiter` and `apps/sadmin` in a single commit. An
  installed binary cannot be rolled forward, and a version prefix cannot be retrofitted after v1.0
  without breaking every phone that has not updated — it costs nothing today and is unbuyable later.
- **One documented error envelope** (`common/http-error-envelope.ts` + the global filter). It codifies
  **Nest's own** `{statusCode, error, message}` rather than inventing one, because
  `HttpException.createBody` already produced exactly that for both the ~121 Zod
  `throw new BadRequestException(parsed.error.issues)` sites and the plain-string throws — so no
  existing web caller changes. The one off-contract path was a thrown **object** body (the apply-quota
  429, returned verbatim with no `statusCode`), fixed **additively** so `ApplyButton`'s reads of
  `upgradeAvailable`/`message` keep working. The Zod issue **array** is deliberately preserved:
  flattening it into one string would break field-level form validation in all three web apps.
- **`/v1/auth/mobile/{register,login,refresh,logout}`** returning tokens in the response body, with the
  refresh token accepted in the request body. A **separate controller** rather than a client-sniffing
  branch on `/auth/*`, so three shipped products are not one bad condition away from a broken login;
  every token decision still lives in `AuthService`, and the same throttles apply — including
  `PerEmailThrottleGuard`, which is not global and would otherwise have left this the one unthrottled
  password endpoint in the app. **Deliberate, owner-approved divergence from CLAUDE.md §9** (HttpOnly
  cookies): a native client has no cookie jar. Mitigations unchanged — HS256, 15-min access, 30-day
  refresh, rotation on every use, `assertSessionAllowed` inside `issueSession`. `expiresIn` is a
  **duration**, never an absolute timestamp: a wrong device clock would otherwise refresh on every call
  or never refresh at all.

**Browser-verified end to end** against real seeded data: mobile login returns a real pair; that Bearer
token answers **200** on `/me/profile`, `/me/saved-jobs`, `/me/applications` and `/me/alerts` (401
without it); refresh rotates and the old token is dead (401 on reuse); logout is idempotent across
valid/repeat/garbage; the envelope holds for the Zod-array, string and 404 shapes.

**Adversarial 5-lens review with three independent skeptics per finding — RUN TWICE.** The first run
reported 23/8, but **7 agents had died on API errors, including the entire route-drift lens** — the
highest-stakes one. Per the standing rule that a run with dead agents is not a pass, it was re-run;
route drift came back **clean**. Across both runs: **51 raised → 9 confirmed (5 distinct), all fixed.**
- **HIGH** — the filter rewrite left the **pre-existing `sentry.filter.test.ts` red**: the new
  `catch()` calls `host.getType()` and that test builds its host as `{} as ArgumentsHost`. Three tests
  threw. The earlier “25/25 pass” was true only of the two new files; the suite had not been run.
- **MED** — the new `Retry-After` was **clobbering ThrottlerGuard's accurate value**. The throttler
  already sets `timeToBlockExpire` before throwing; an unconditional `setHeader` replaced a real
  decaying countdown with a flat 60 on every throttler 429 in the API, making the exact signal this
  branch advertises for mobile back-off *less* accurate than develop. Now fills gaps only — measured
  live decaying **47 → 41 → 35**.
- **MED** — unhandled 500s were **logged nowhere**. `BaseExceptionFilter.handleUnknownError` logs every
  bare Error; writing the response ourselves skipped it. With `SENTRY_DSN` blank (the documented
  local-dev setup) a 500 became completely silent — no stack, nothing in Sentry, and a body
  deliberately stripped of the message; in prod, flipping `killswitch.telemetry` would also have
  blinded the logs. Restored, deliberately **not** gated on the killswitch.
- **MED** — `retryAfterSeconds` **lied about two other 429s**, guessing 60s for a **1-hour**
  `PerEmailThrottleGuard` lockout and a possibly **monthly** recruiter post quota; a client honouring
  that retries across the whole window and re-trips it. It now answers only when the body says
  something definite (the recruiter quota's explicit `window` discriminator, or the apply quota's
  top-level `limit`) and returns **null** otherwise, because a wrong `Retry-After` is worse than none.
  `PerEmailThrottleGuard` now emits its own accurate header from the Redis key's TTL.
- **MED** — body-parser **http-errors collapsed to 500**. `entity.too.large` (413 — `express.json`
  defaults to 100kb), `request.aborted` (400) and `charset.unsupported` (415) reach this filter as
  Express error middleware and used to keep their status via `isHttpError`. They were answering 500,
  *and* being captured to Sentry as 5xx — inflating precisely the server-error rate the observability
  work exists to measure. Verified live: a 200KB POST now returns a real **413**.

**API 812 → 858 tests** (+46). Gate green on the integrated state: typecheck **12/12** · tests **9/9**
· build **5/5**. Note: the gate initially failed on `packages/db/src/sequence.test.ts` — a teammate's
file, not this branch — because `vitest` was declared in `packages/db/package.json` but missing from
its `node_modules` (the OneDrive de-materialization hazard). `pnpm install` restored it.

**Next per ADR 0002:** the shared-domain extraction PR (step 4), then `GET /jobs` + `/jobs/:slug`.
**Still owner-blocked and unchanged:** nothing is deployed anywhere (no host, no CI, no managed
Elasticsearch), `RESEND_API_KEY` is blank, and the store-compliance surfaces (privacy policy page,
account-deletion endpoint) do not exist.


### PR — `feature/domain-package-extraction` · 2026-08-08 — `@jobportal/domain` (ADR 0002 step 4)

**Why this had to come first.** The CQ mobile spec listed nine endpoints and no extraction phase. Six
of them depend on the same five modules in `apps/web/lib`, and `apps/api` **cannot import `apps/web`
at all** — no dependency in `apps/api/package.json`, no alias in `tsconfig.base.json`, `include`
scoped to `src/**/*.ts`. Built in the spec's order, every endpoint PR would have copy-pasted what it
needed and the repo would have ended with the canonical-slug parser, the job-visibility rules and the
SRP param mapping living in two places each, silently drifting from the website forever.

**What moved** (via `git mv`, so history follows each file), together with its test file:

| From | To | Unblocks |
|---|---|---|
| `lib/url/slug.ts` | `src/slug.ts` | `/jobs/:slug`, `/companies/:handle` |
| `lib/job/visibility.ts` | `src/job-visibility.ts` | `/jobs/:slug` |
| `lib/srp/params.ts` | `src/srp-params.ts` | `/jobs` |
| `lib/companies/params.ts` | `src/company-params.ts` | `/companies` |
| `lib/cms/params.ts` | `src/article-params.ts` | `/career-advice` |
| `lib/home/queries.ts` | `src/home-queries.ts` | `/home` |

**Behaviour-preserving, with an invariant that proves it.** No query, endpoint, route, output or
rendered page changed. `apps/web` went **216 → 138** tests and the new package reports **78** —
summing to **exactly 216**, so nothing was lost, skipped or duplicated in the move.

**The one module that needed surgery.** `home-queries.ts` wrapped its loader in React's `cache()`.
That helper is **RSC-only and inert outside a React request**, so it cannot live in a package shared
with NestJS. The package now exports the **uncached** loader; `apps/web/lib/home/queries.ts` is a thin
`cache()` wrapper at the original path. It stays because it is load-bearing *for the website
specifically* — several server components call `loadHomePageData()` in the same render, and without
the dedup each would re-run the whole 10-query aggregate.

**A bug the tests caught that typecheck could not.** Subpath imports like
`@jobportal/domain/srp-params` typechecked cleanly — TypeScript resolves through `tsconfig` paths —
and then **failed at runtime**, because vitest and Node resolve through `package.json`. Fixed with an
explicit **named** `exports` map (not a `./*` wildcard), matching `@jobportal/ui` and
`@jobportal/observability`, so the public surface is documented and internals cannot be reached. Worth
remembering: on this repo, a green typecheck does not prove a new package resolves.

**22 import sites rewritten rather than shimmed**, so no permanent indirection is left behind; two
barrels (`lib/url/index.ts`, `lib/srp/index.ts`) forward to the package so their consumers are
untouched. Two imports used a sibling path (`'../srp/params'`) and were missed by the first pass —
typecheck caught them.

**Deliberately not moved:** `apps/web/lib/cms/markdown.ts`. It is the Shiki/unified pipeline, ESM-only
against a CommonJS API build, and the owner's ADR 0002 decision 3 (return raw markdown to the mobile
client) removes the reason to share it.

**Browser-verified** on every surface these modules power: home, SRP with filters, companies with
`category`+`hiring`, career advice with search — all **200**. Job detail **200**, slug drift **308**,
malformed slug **404**, unknown id **404**; company handle **200** and its drift **308**. All 23 job
hrefs on the SRP end in a numeric id per CLAUDE.md §5. **Zero console errors.**

**Adversarial 5-lens review with three independent skeptics per finding — 65/65 agents, no errors:
20 raised → 1 confirmed.** ARCHITECTURE.md still described the old seven-package layout in both the
monorepo tree and the shared-packages table, so a reader had no way to learn the package exists —
precisely the failure it was created to prevent. Fixed, with a callout for the `cache()` gotcha.
Separately, `@jobportal/domain` was added to `apps/web`'s `transpilePackages`: the review raised this
**five times and refuted it 3/3 every time**, correctly — it demonstrably works, the production build
passes and every page renders. It was added anyway because `next.config.ts` states the rule in its own
comment and `domain` was the only one of eight workspace dependencies missing from the list.

**Gate green on the integrated state:** typecheck **13/13** (12 → 13 workspaces) · tests **10/10**
(API 858 / sadmin 216 / **web 138** / recruiter 83 / **domain 78, new** / feature-flags 37 / ui 27 /
db 20 / observability 19 / auth 17) · build **5/5**. **On pull run `pnpm install`** — there is a new
workspace package.

**Next per ADR 0002:** `GET /jobs` + `GET /jobs/:slug`, now unblocked — the biggest single win for the
app. **Still owner-blocked and unchanged:** nothing is deployed (no host, no CI, no managed
Elasticsearch), `RESEND_API_KEY` is blank, and the store-compliance surfaces do not exist.


### PR — `feature/public-jobs-api` · 2026-08-08 — public job browse (ADR 0002 step 5)

The endpoint pair the mobile spec called the core of the app, plus the bulk lookup that had to ship
with it. `apps/api` only — no schema, no migration, no new flag key, no shared-surface lock.

**What shipped**
- **`GET /v1/jobs`** — paginated, ACTIVE-only search with the full SRP filter set.
- **`GET /v1/jobs/:slug`** — detail with optional auth and a 308 on slug drift.
- **`POST /v1/me/job-state`** — authenticated bulk saved/applied lookup. Deliberately in the same PR:
  without it a 20-card page needs 20 extra round trips to draw its save icons, and adding it later
  would have changed the list response shape after clients were written against it.

**Reuse, not reimplementation — the extraction PR paying off.** The Elasticsearch query is
`searchJobs` from `@jobportal/search`, the same function the website's SRP calls. Param mapping goes
through `@jobportal/domain/srp-params`, slug parsing through `/slug`, and the visibility decision
through `/job-visibility`. "expMin=2" and "is this job public" now mean the identical thing on both
surfaces by construction, not by discipline.

**Security, in two layers.** `status` is absent from the `.strict()` DTO (so it 400s) *and* pinned to
ACTIVE after the spread in the service — `searchJobs` only DEFAULTS to ACTIVE and the param is
caller-overridable, so forwarding raw query params would have served DRAFT documents out of the index.

**Ordering is load-bearing on the detail route.** The visibility check runs BEFORE the canonical 308,
because the redirect's `Location` header carries the real title-bearing slug — redirecting first
would disclose an unapproved job's title to anyone who guessed its id. Verified against a
temporarily-`PENDING_MODERATION` job: anonymous gets a **404 with no Location header**, byte-identical
to the 404 an unknown id gets, while an admin Bearer token gets 200. **The row was restored and
verified byte-identically afterwards.**

**New `OptionalJwtAuthGuard`** — the non-throwing sibling of `JwtAuthGuard`, so a public route can
recognise an owner/collaborator/admin previewing an unpublished job while a missing *or malformed*
token simply means anonymous. Reusing the strict guard would have turned a bad token into a 401 on a
public page. Verified: a garbage Bearer gets 404 on a hidden job and 200 on the public list.

**Adversarial 5-lens review with three skeptics per finding — 86/86 agents, no errors: 27 raised →
5 confirmed.** All five describe one root defect, and its second facet was in the error envelope
shipped in the *previous* PR — this branch is what made it reachable.

`searchJobs` was awaited with no error handling, and `@elastic/transport`'s `ResponseError` carries
`statusCode: number` + `message: string`: precisely the duck-type the global filter used to recognise
an http-error. So an Elasticsearch failure on the API's **first unauthenticated route** (a) answered
with Elasticsearch's own status, (b) echoed its raw exception text — naming the engine and its
internal index settings — to anyone, and (c) was classified as an "expected 4xx" and therefore
**never captured by Sentry**, so a real outage would have been invisible in monitoring. The trigger
was baked into this branch's own DTO: `page` was capped at 1000 with a comment rationalising it, but
with `PAGE_SIZE` 20 and ES's default `index.max_result_window` of 10000 the last servable page is
**500** — pages 501–1000 were inside the advertised contract and always failed, loudly.

Fixed three ways: the page bound is now **derived** (`MAX_PAGE = ES_MAX_RESULT_WINDOW / PAGE_SIZE`)
so it cannot drift if either constant moves; ES failures become a clean **503** carrying the original
as `cause`, so the detail reaches our logs and Sentry without reaching the client, and 503 being
≥ 500 means it *is* captured; and the filter now requires a **boolean `expose`** to recognise an
http-error — verified against the installed packages that http-errors sets it true on 4xx and false
on 5xx while ES's ResponseError leaves it undefined. The status is still adopted for any genuine
http-error (a 502 really is a 502); only the **message** is gated on `expose`, which is exactly what
the library documents the flag for.

Worth recording: fixing this broke three filter tests from the previous PR whose fixtures built
http-errors **without** `expose` — unrealistic fixtures rather than a behaviour regression — and
repairing them surfaced that the first attempt also dropped the status of genuine 5xx http-errors.
The final split (status always, message only when exposed) preserves develop's behaviour on every
path that was already correct.

**Verified live**, including by actually stopping the `jp-elasticsearch` container: a real outage
returns `503 {"message":"Job search is temporarily unavailable"}` with no internals, and the ES
client recovers on its own once the container returns — no API restart needed. Filters (sort, city,
multi-city, skill, experience range, postedWithin, salaryMin, page), the full detail shape, slug
drift 308 with the canonical slug in the body so a client that does not follow redirects can
self-correct, and the job-state lookup returning real applications for a seeded candidate.

**Found while verifying, NOT introduced here:** the local Elasticsearch index was stale — an EXPIRED
job was still indexed as ACTIVE and so appeared in search. The **website shows the identical row**,
confirming a pre-existing dev-data artifact rather than a regression; the expiry sweep does call
`syncJob`. `pnpm --filter @jobportal/search search:reindex` fixed it locally (43 jobs indexed,
matching the ACTIVE count exactly). **Teammates may see the same thing on their machines.**

**API 858 → 899 tests** (+41). Gate green on the integrated state: typecheck **13/13** · tests
**10/10** · build **5/5**.

**Next per ADR 0002:** the reference catalogs (`/skills`, `/cities`, `/industries`), then companies,
home, career advice, and the applications extras — followed by the API contract document the app
team needs to build against while hosting is deferred. **Still owner-blocked:** nothing is deployed
(the owner has deferred hosting deliberately), `RESEND_API_KEY` is blank, and the store-compliance
surfaces do not exist.


### PR — `feature/public-catalogs-api` · 2026-08-08 — reference catalogs (ADR 0002 step 6)

`GET /v1/skills`, `GET /v1/cities`, `GET /v1/industries`. Public, unauthenticated, one controller
over the three seed tables. **Promoted ahead of companies**, and the reason matters: the mobile spec
framed these as filter pickers, but they are a hard blocker for the app's **profile screen**.
`GET /me/profile` returns bare ids with no names — verified against the seeded candidate, it really
does answer `skillIds [7,110,62,47,80]` and `preferredCityIds [1,4]` and nothing else — so the app
cannot draw that screen at all without resolving them.

Hence an **`?ids=` resolve mode** alongside the usual search and paging:
`?ids=7,110,62,47,80` → *Distributed Systems, Go, Kafka, Kubernetes, PostgreSQL*; `?ids=1,4` →
*Bangalore (Karnataka), Hyderabad (Telangana)*. Without it the client would download the whole
catalogue or issue one request per id. Resolve mode **ignores `q` and `page`** deliberately —
paginating a lookup would silently drop ids the caller asked about, leaving a profile with missing
chips and no signal anything was lost — de-duplicates, caps at 100, and **omits an id that no longer
exists rather than 404ing**, so a stale skill id on an old profile cannot make the profile
unrenderable.

Three judgment calls, all recorded rather than assumed: **`?q=`** adds server-side case-insensitive
name search, a *superset* of the website (whose pickers download the entire table and filter
client-side — fine on a desktop, wrong on Indian mobile data); **`pageSize` is client-settable here
and only here** (default 20, cap 100), a deliberate divergence from the fixed 20 the feed endpoints
use, because a picker wants the small table in one round trip; and **custom skills are not filtered
out**, matching the SSR pickers, because hiding them here would silently diverge from the site —
worth a product conversation if junk accumulates, not a call to take unilaterally inside an API meant
to mirror the website.

**Adversarial 4-lens review with three skeptics per finding — 67/67 agents, no errors: 21 raised →
1 confirmed HIGH.** Following its fix hint turned one instance into **five**.

Every `id` column in this schema is a Prisma `Int` (Postgres int4). Handing Prisma a larger number
does **not** match zero rows — it **throws**, escaping as an unhandled 500. On an unauthenticated
route that means an anonymous caller generates 5xx responses and Sentry events by adding one digit to
a URL. Reproduced before the fix: `?ids=2147483648`, `?ids=1e10` (Number.isInteger(1e10) is true, so
the exponent form slipped past the digit check), `?ids=3000000000`, the authenticated `jobIds` batch,
**and the already-merged public `GET /v1/jobs/x-2147483648`** — all 500. All now answer 400, or 404
for the slug, with the boundary value 2147483647 behaving exactly as before.

The slug guard lives in **`@jobportal/domain`**, so it repairs the **website and the API together**:
the SSR job/company pages and `GET /v1/jobs/:slug` funnel through the same parsers, and
`parseCompanySlug`/`parseWorkingAtSlug` had the identical hole. `apps/api` gets a small shared
`int32` helper rather than a magic number repeated per DTO. Rejecting beats clamping — a caller
asking about id 3000000000 has asked a malformed question, not one whose answer is 2147483647.

**domain 78 → 81, API 899 → 919 tests.** Gate green on the integrated state: typecheck **13/13** ·
tests **10/10** · build **5/5**.

**Next per ADR 0002:** companies, home, career advice, the applications extras, then the API contract
document the app team needs while hosting is deferred. **Still owner-blocked:** hosting (deliberately
deferred), `RESEND_API_KEY`, and the store-compliance surfaces.

## PR log

Most recent first. Each entry: PR number, branch, SRS section, one-paragraph summary of what was actually shipped, plus any deliberate deferrals or follow-ups.

### `feature/sadmin-candidate-management` — the Candidate Master List, and Candidate management in the rail · 2026-08-03

**SRS §4.16** (admin console). CLI merge to `develop` (`--no-ff`). **`apps/sadmin` only** — `apps/api`, `apps/web`, `apps/recruiter`, `apps/services` and every `packages/*` verified byte-untouched. **No schema change, no migration, no new flag key, no `theme.css` token, no barrel edit, no shared-surface lock taken. Nothing to run on pull.**
