**What shipped.** A new `Candidate management` item in the Super Admin rail, directly below `Employer management`, rendering `/sadmin/candidates`: a read-only master list of every registered job seeker, newest first, 20 per page, with a debounced search bar. Columns are Candidate (initials-monogram avatar + name), Headline, Contact (email over phone), Location, Registered, and Actions. The page is a server component reading Postgres directly through Prisma, mirroring `/employers` — the repo's reads/writes split, where only mutations go through `apps/api`.

**The load-bearing decision: this queries `User`, not `Candidate`.** `prisma.candidate.findMany()` is the obvious implementation and it is wrong. The `Candidate` profile row is provisioned **lazily** on the first `/profile` read; email+password registration creates only the `User`; and the Google signup path swallows a failed `Candidate` create. Any of the three leaves a genuinely registered seeker with no `Candidate` row, so the obvious query would have silently omitted them from a list whose whole purpose is completeness — no error, no warning, just missing people. The dashboard's "Job seekers" KPI counts `user.count({ where: { role: 'CANDIDATE' } })` for exactly this reason and an existing test pins it. `candidate` is therefore a nullable relation everywhere in the mapping.

**Column mapping was verified field-by-field against the schema rather than assumed**, and one column genuinely has no backing field: the profile picture. `User.image` is the only avatar column in the entire schema and is documented as an OAuth-provider URL, so it is null for every email/password account — including all 20 demo candidates. Rather than invent a migration, this was surfaced to the owner as a scope decision; the owner chose to ship `User.image` with an initials-monogram fallback, accepting that the monogram is the dominant visual case. Radix's `Avatar` also degrades to it when a stored Google URL has expired, which a bare `<img>` would render as a broken-image glyph. `next/image` is a trap on this surface and is deliberately avoided: basePath is not applied to a string `src`, and `next.config.ts` declares no `images.remotePatterns`.

**Search** matches name and email, case-insensitively — the owner picked that scope over also including phone (stored unnormalised, so `+91 98765` would miss `09876543210`) or headline (needs a relation join and misses seekers with no `Candidate` row). It is a client island rather than a GET `<form>` because this app sets basePath `/sadmin`, which next/link and the router apply but a raw HTML form `action` does not — a form would submit to the origin root and 404. The where-clause is built **once** and passed by reference to both the `findMany` and the `count`: the employer list gets away with a bare `count()` only because it has no filter, and here a divergence would make the total, the count line, the pagination and the over-range redirect all disagree with the visible rows.

**Actions render inert, as scoped.** They use `aria-disabled` rather than the `disabled` attribute so they stay in the tab order and are announced as unavailable instead of being invisible to keyboard users; they carry no `onClick`, so from a server component they ship zero JavaScript and the click is a genuine no-op; they are not dimmed with `opacity` (which drops 14px muted text below legibility); and **Delete is deliberately not red**, because colour would promise a destructive action that cannot happen yet. ⚠️ **Suspend cannot be wired at all without a migration** — there is no `status` / `suspendedAt` / `deactivatedAt` / `isActive` / `deletedAt` column on `User` or `Candidate`. The shape to mirror is `Recruiter.deactivatedAt`, and making it real needs that column plus a new `ProfileAuditAction` enum member, session revocation, a login block, and an endpoint in `apps/api` behind `AdminGuard` — none of which is in this PR.

**Browser verification caught a defect before the review ran.** With `title="Not available yet"` on the inert buttons, the browser's own accessibility tree named **all sixty controls** "Not available yet" — `title` beat the button's content, so View, Suspend and Delete were indistinguishable to a screen reader and the visible word was lost from the name entirely. Confirmed with a controlled three-button probe injected into the live page (content+title → "Not available yet"; content only → "View"; title only → "Not available yet"). Fixed with an explicit `aria-label`, which outranks both, starting with the visible word so voice control still matches "click View" (WCAG 2.5.3).

**Adversarial 5-lens review (data · react · a11y · nextjs · conventions), three independent skeptics per finding, majority-refute kills it: 11 raised → 6 confirmed across 4 distinct defects, 5 refuted. All fixed.**

- **HIGH — search results changed silently for screen readers.** The island was copied from `apps/recruiter/components/jobs/JobsFilterBar.tsx` but not the half that makes it perceivable: that page pairs the bar with `<p role="status">` carrying the comment *"the new match count is announced when filters change"*. Here the count line and the empty state were inert paragraphs. Because the bar commits with `router.replace(..., { scroll: false })`, focus never moves and the pathname and `<title>` never change — so Next's route announcer, which diffs the title, says nothing either. Narrowing 1,240 rows to zero was announced by **nothing at all**, and the admin would keep believing the old results were on screen. Fixed with **one always-mounted `role="status"`** whose text switches between the count and the empty copy: a live region that mounts *together with* its message does not announce, a trap already documented in this repo at `RevealCodeButton` and `VerifiableField`. Browser-verified that React updates it in place — **same DOM node** before and after, text swapped, role retained.
- **MED — a repeated `?q` crashed the route.** Next delivers `searchParams` as `string | string[] | undefined`, so `?q=a&q=b` reached `raw.trim()` on an array. **Reproduced in the dev server**: `TypeError: raw.trim is not a function` at `format.ts:50`, the whole Candidate management route replaced by the error boundary. The sibling `page` param survived the same input purely by accident, because `clampPage` funnels through `Number(...)` and `Number(['1','2'])` is `NaN`. Fixed with `firstParam` — the guard `apps/recruiter`'s jobs page already uses — applied to **both** params, and `PageProps` retyped as Next actually delivers it.
- **LOW — the avatar monogram was announced before every name.** Radix renders the fallback as a plain `<span>` with no `aria-hidden` of its own, and the initials are derived from the very name rendered beside it, so each row read as *"P S Priya Sharma"* — twenty junk tokens per page. The control is purely decorative, so `aria-hidden` now sits on the root, the same treatment the `(authed)` layout gives its identical account-row monogram.

**Refuted and deliberately not changed** (each by 3/3 skeptics): `%`/`_` behaving as LIKE wildcards in `contains` (the house pattern — four merged call sites do the same with raw user input); the shell's navigation veil firing on a debounced search (the shared loader's intended contract, and the precedent behaves identically); clear-button icon contrast; and two findings about `initials` duplication and a `PROGRESS.md` entry that this branch writes last by design.

**Gate green on the integrated state**: **typecheck 12/12 · tests 9/9 (1,447 total; sadmin 175 → 216) · build 5/5**. `/candidates` builds as `ƒ` Dynamic, matching `/employers`.

**Browser-verified end-to-end** against the real dev database: the rail item sits directly below Employer management with `aria-current="page"`; 20 rows render with monograms, populated headlines and em-dashes for the absent phone and location; search filters to the right row while **keeping focus and caret position**; the empty state says *"No candidates match X"* rather than the "nobody has registered" lie; `?page=99` and `?q=a&page=99` both return a genuine server **307** preserving `q` (never the soft 200 a `loading.tsx` would cause — which is why this segment deliberately has none); pagination was exercised by temporarily inserting 5 extra rows, giving **20 + 5 with zero overlap across the page seam** — which also proves the `id` tiebreak in the `orderBy`, since those five rows were inserted in one statement and share a `createdAt` to the millisecond; the phone column was confirmed rendering as written via one probe row; 12/12 contrast samples pass (min 7.14, canvas-composited and sanity-checked at exactly 21:1); the scrolling table needs no `tabIndex` because it contains 60 focusable controls; no horizontal overflow at 1280 or 375; and 0 console errors. **The 5 probe rows were deleted and the database verified back to its exact baseline (32 users, 20 candidates, 0 probes).**

**FOLLOW-UPS**: (1) wire View / Suspend / Delete — View needs a `/candidates/[id]` detail route, Suspend needs the `User.deactivatedAt` migration described above, and **all three must go through `apps/api`**, never a server action in this RSC, or they bypass `AdminGuard`, Zod validation and audit logging; (2) `User.name` and `User.createdAt` are unindexed, so the search is a role-filtered scan plus a sort — fine at this scale, worth a trigram index long before it matters.

### `bugfix/seed-sequence-monotonic` — the demo seeds rewound `User_id_seq` under live rows and broke signup · 2026-08-03 (work 2026-07-29)

No SRS section — a seed / data-integrity bugfix. **Registration was returning 500 with Prisma `P2002` on `User_pkey`, and the demo seeds were the cause.** Both seeds insert rows with explicit ids (`Job` 100001-100050 in `demo.ts`, `User` 200001-200020 in `demo-applications.ts`), which Postgres does **not** count as sequence usage, so each ended with a `setval()` to push the sequence past its own range. That `setval` was **an unconditional assignment to a compile-time constant derived from the seed's own array length** — `setval(pg_get_serial_sequence('"User"','id'), 200020, true)`, with no `GREATEST`, no `max(id)`, no guard of any kind. Every row above that constant was invisible to it: real accounts created by registering through the app, and the super-admin from `seed/admin.ts`, which uses plain autoincrement *precisely* to stay clear of the demo range — its comment at `admin.ts:104` shows the author saw the hazard, and the `setval` is the half that never got fixed. So each re-run of `pnpm db:seed:demo:full` moved the sequence **backwards below existing rows**, and `nextval()` then handed out ids that were already taken until it climbed back past `max(id)`. **Nothing catches that P2002**: `auth.service.ts` pre-checks only email uniqueness (that is the 409 path), so a primary-key collision falls through `SentryGlobalFilter` as a plain 500 — recruiter *and* candidate signup were both dead.

**Field state, 2026-07-29**: `User_id_seq.last_value` 200021 against `max(User.id)` 200023 (200021-200022 real recruiter signups, 200023 the seeded admin). The same database also had **`Job_id_seq` at 100053 against `max(Job.id)` 100240** — the identical rewind on the other sequence, latent only because ids 100054-100238 happened to be free; it would have posted 186 more jobs and then started colliding. A repo-wide sweep confirmed these are the **only two** sequence-manipulation sites that exist: 31 migrations contain `SERIAL` DDL and nothing else, and of all 44 models (every one an `Int @id @default(autoincrement())` — there are no cuid/uuid PKs) only those two are seeded with explicit ids, so no table is missing a `setval` either.

Both call sites now go through **`advanceSequence()`** (`packages/db/src/sequence.ts`), which resolves the sequence via `pg_get_serial_sequence` and then sets it to `GREATEST(max(id), current sequence value, caller's floor)` in **a single statement**, so it can only ever move a sequence forward. Two details are load-bearing: it reads the current position with **`pg_sequence_last_value()`, not `last_value`** — the former returns NULL on a never-called sequence, which is genuinely "nothing handed out yet", whereas `last_value` reads 1 and would burn id 1 on a fresh database; and it pairs `GREATEST(target, 1)` with `is_called := target >= 1` so an empty table still gets id 1 rather than skipping it. Table/column names are interpolated (Postgres cannot parameterise an identifier) and are therefore regex-restricted to a bare identifier shape. The helper lives in `src/`, not next to the seeds, for a specific reason: **`packages/db/tsconfig.json` includes only `src/**/*.ts` and `prisma.config.ts`, so the entire `prisma/` tree — every seed module — is silently untypechecked**, which is a large part of how this survived.

**First vitest suite in `packages/db`** (`pnpm test` now fans out to 8 workspaces, not 7): 20 tests — 13 unit (identifier guards including quote-break-out attempts, statement shape, `bigint`/string driver coercion, precision refusal) and 7 integration against a real local Postgres on a throwaway `_seq_advance_probe_<pid>` table. The regression is covered end-to-end — seed explicit ids → advance → two real id-less inserts → re-seed → assert the sequence did not drop and the next insert still succeeds — and reverting `advanceSequence` to the old unconditional `setval` turns 4 of the 20 red, so the suite is not vacuous. A companion test characterises the removed shape directly, asserting that the bare `setval` still produces the duplicate-key failure. **Two gates guard the integration block, and they answer different questions**: the seeds' URL predicate (plus a `NODE_ENV` check) asks whether the database is *safe to write to*, and a connection probe asks whether anything is *listening* — conflating those is how a suite goes red on a laptop with Docker stopped, so an unreachable server skips the block with the reason on stderr instead of failing in `beforeAll`. The fixture table is per-process, so two runs against one database (two worktrees, or a watcher beside `pnpm test`) no longer drop each other's table mid-test.

Verified against the live dev database through the real `PrismaClient`, not just the test's `pg` connection: the call **repaired the rewound `Job_id_seq` in place (100053 → 100240)** and correctly left `User_id_seq` alone (already at 200025, above `max(id)` 200023), with a second call reporting `moved: false`. **No schema change, no migration, no new flag key, no shared-surface lock** — `packages/db` only. **Nothing to run on pull**; the next `pnpm db:seed:demo` / `:apps` repairs the sequence on any machine that already has a rewound one.

**Adversarial 4-lens review** (Postgres semantics · TypeScript · test quality · repo fit), one independent skeptic per finding, every claim executed against the live PG 18.4 rather than argued: **12 raised → 2 confirmed, 6 refuted, 4 low-severity left unverified and judged by hand**. Both confirmed defects were in the *test*, not the fix, and both are now closed: the suite **failed instead of skipping when Postgres was unreachable** (a URL that merely looks local says nothing about a server listening — measured `exit 1` with Docker stopped, on the repo's only pre-merge gate), and the fixture table was a **hardcoded name that two concurrent runs would drop out from under each other**. Refuted with evidence: that the anti-vacuity test does not bite (a revert reddens 4 tests), that `pg_sequence_last_value` returning NULL after `ALTER SEQUENCE … RESTART WITH` could cause a rewind (the `max(id)` operand still floors it), and twice that testing through raw `pg` rather than `PrismaClient` leaves a real gap (Prisma 7 *is* `PrismaPg` here, and both `bigint` and string coercion paths are pinned — the helper was also exercised through a real `PrismaClient` against the dev database by hand). Three accuracy nits from the unverified set were taken anyway: `after`/`moved` now report the computed high-water mark rather than `setval`'s echo, so an empty table reads `{before: 0, after: 0, moved: false}` instead of claiming it moved; the comment about `pg_sequence_last_value` returning NULL now says *`is_called = false`*, which is the real condition; and the claim that one statement prevents interleaving is softened to what it actually buys, since it takes no table lock.

**Deferrals / follow-ups** (both filed as separate tasks, deliberately out of this PR): (1) the `prisma/` tree is excluded from `tsc --noEmit`, and probing with it included surfaces **3 pre-existing errors** — two `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` failures in `articles.ts` and, more interestingly, `flags.ts:78` seeding a flag with category `"moderation"`, which is **not in the allowed union**; (2) `recruiter-jobs.service.ts:316-330` consumes a posting-quota slot before `tx.job.create()` and its "refund" path only writes a log line, so every failed post permanently burns a paid slot — which this bug was triggering repeatedly.

### `bugfix/forgot-password-otp` — RPT-008: reset by OTP, and the Reset Ledger · 2026-07-30

**Tester report RPT-008** (SRS §4.12.5). CLI merge to `develop` (`--no-ff`). `apps/api` + `apps/web` + one schema change; `apps/recruiter`, `apps/sadmin`, `apps/services` and `packages/ui` untouched.

**Two complaints, two different causes — worth separating, because only one was a code defect.** (1) *“Not receiving the email.”* Reproduced and traced to configuration: `RESEND_API_KEY` is blank, so `ResendClient` logs `(stub — Resend not configured)` and returns. That silently drops **every** transactional email — password reset, email verification, recruiter invites, application confirmations, job alerts — and no amount of code fixes it. The owner chose to defer provisioning the key and accept a **strictly dev-gated** fallback (`NODE_ENV !== 'production'` AND no API key) that logs the code so testers are unblocked locally; it is dead code the moment either condition changes. **⚠️ FOLLOW-UP: a real Resend key is required before launch.** (2) *“It sends a link where other platforms send an OTP.”* Correct, and by design — so the flow was reworked.

**The flow.** `POST /auth/forgot-password` issues a code and answers 200 with `{expiresAt, resendAvailableAt, expiresInSeconds, resendInSeconds}`; a new `POST /auth/verify-reset-otp` exchanges a correct code for a one-time ticket; `POST /auth/reset-password` spends the ticket, sets the password, revokes every other session and **mints a session** — the reset ends signed in (the owner’s explicit call: the caller has just proven mailbox control *and* chosen the password, so a login form adds friction without adding proof).

**Schema** (lock taken and released). `PasswordResetToken` gains `codeHash` / `attempts` / `resendCount` / `lastSentAt` / `verifiedAt`; `tokenHash` becomes the post-verification ticket and loses NOT NULL; `@@index([userId])` is **swapped for** `@@unique([userId])` so a resend REPLACES the code in place rather than leaving N valid codes standing. **The migration is not purely additive** — it DELETEs every existing row, because a link token has no code to verify and `codeHash` is NOT NULL; outstanding reset links stop working, which is the correct outcome of retiring the link flow.

**Security, since six digits is a 10^6 space a script exhausts trivially.** The attempt cap is claimed by a conditional UPDATE *before* the comparison, so it fails CLOSED under concurrency instead of handing every racer a free guess; codes are hashed at rest salted with `userId` and compared in constant time; the DTO rejects non-6-digit input so junk cannot burn a victim’s attempts; the send gate is serialised by a transaction-scoped advisory lock (the device `RecruiterOtpService` already uses); and **nothing becomes an enumeration oracle** — unknown addresses, OAuth-only accounts, active cooldowns and spent resend budgets all return the identical shape and never throw, because a 429 only real accounts receive would itself be the leak. One asymmetry is documented rather than hidden: a wrong guess against a real account says “N attempts left”, which `POST /auth/register` already exposes more cheaply by answering 409 on a taken address.

**UI — “The Reset Ledger”.** Chosen by the owner from a judge-panel design workflow (4 concepts → 3 judges → 2 shortlisted) presented as one interactive full-fidelity Artifact; a third concept was cut on a fact the panel checked — a navy-panel-left/form-right layout would have cloned the recruiter app’s shipped `AuthSplit`. All three steps are on screen from first paint and finished ones collapse to a receipt row carrying the fact they proved. It earns its keep by **deleting a control**: there is no Back button, because `Change` lives permanently on the collapsed email row — which is also the only exit from a real trap, since a mistyped address receives the generic error on every guess and would otherwise loop forever. The OTP field is **one real input under six painted wells** (autofill, paste, assistive tech and native text editing all target a single control; paste prefers the LAST run of six digits, so “Career Queue 2026 — your code is 483920” cannot yield “202648”). New `ResetLedger`/`LedgerStep`/`OtpField`/`FormError` (ported from `apps/recruiter`)/`lib/auth/password-rules`; `PasswordInput` gains OPTIONAL controlled visibility so one eye drives both password fields while `LoginForm`/`RegisterForm` stay untouched; `/reset-password` redirects.

**Adversarial 4-lens review — and a process note.** The first run returned “0 findings”, but all four agents had failed with API 529s; that is an infrastructure failure and was **not** treated as a pass. Re-run properly: **28 raised → 21 confirmed (13 distinct), all fixed.** **HIGH:** a soft-deactivated recruiter could regain a session — `login()` blocks them, but the check lived *in* `login()` while `issueSession` is shared by login, Google OAuth and now the reset, and the recruiter jobs/applicants controllers authorise on the JWT role claim without re-checking `deactivatedAt`; moving the gate into `issueSession` closes the reset path **and** the Google path that predates this branch. **MED:** `resetWithTicket` did not fail closed — Prisma’s array-form `$transaction` runs every statement and commits unless one *rejects*, and `updateMany` matching zero rows resolves, so the guard only shaped the HTTP response while the password write and session revocation had already committed (the loser of a double-submit changed the password and signed the winner out while being told it failed) → interactive transaction. **MED:** the code was not single-use — `codeHash` survived and `verifiedAt` was never re-checked, so the same digits could be re-verified to mint fresh tickets, silently voiding the held one, and stayed redeemable past the advertised 15 minutes. **MED:** the tests could not have caught either, because `$transaction` was a bare `vi.fn()` that never ran the callback; the mock now models real semantics and asserts that *nothing* is written on the losing path. Plus a read-then-write race on the send gate, `stale` ignoring `usedAt` (a completed reset gated the next one), no focus management on step changes, errors never associated with their controls, one `busy` flag driving three requests, countdowns comparing server timestamps to the device clock (a fast clock declared a live code expired → the API now returns durations and the client counts elapsed local time), `<div>` inside `<p>` in the email template, and an `@@index` comment promising a purge sweep that does not exist.

**Gate:** typecheck **12/12** · tests **7/7** (**API 812** / web 216 / recruiter 53 / sadmin 86 / auth 17 / feature-flags 37 / observability 19) · build **5/5**. **Verified end-to-end in the browser and against the API**: the happy path lands signed in on `/profile`; a wrong code shows “4 attempts left” with red wells but **dark digits**; an unknown address gets the generic message; a malformed code is rejected **without** spending an attempt; the code is single-use; ticket replay is rejected; a fresh request right after a completed reset works; the countdown decrements locally (14:58→14:56); focus moves into each new step; every `aria-describedby` id resolves; **0 contrast failures** and non-text marks clear 1.4.11; no overflow at 1280/375; 44px tap targets; 0 console errors. **DB restored byte-identically** after each run (three users’ password hashes saved and compared, reset rows cleared). **FOLLOW-UPS:** provision `RESEND_API_KEY`; add a purge sweep for expired reset rows.

### `feature/recruiter-signup-otp-verification` — OTP-verified email + mobile at recruiter signup, and the sadmin "OTP Sessions" relay console · 2026-07-29

SRS §4.9 / §4.12. A recruiter can no longer create an account until **both** their email address and their Indian mobile number have been proved by a 6-digit one-time code. There is deliberately **no SMS or email provider**: codes live in the database and a Career Queue staff member reads them off a new `/sadmin/otp-sessions` page and relays them by phone or WhatsApp. **On pull run `pnpm db:generate && pnpm db:migrate:dev`** (two migrations).

**Schema** (2 migrations, lock taken and released): `add_recruiter_signup_otp_verification` (enum `OtpChannel`, model `OtpChallenge`, `User.phoneVerified`, `ProfileAuditAction.OTP_CODE_REVEALED`) and a corrective `rename_otp_challenge_consumed_to_verified`. `OtpChallenge` has **no FK to User** — the challenge is issued before the account exists — and is correlated by a server-minted 32-byte `signupId`, with `@@unique([signupId, channel])` making "one live challenge per channel" a real database guarantee. **`User.phone` was reused, not duplicated**: it already exists and is already written by candidate signup, so a parallel `Recruiter.phone` would have been the duplicate column COLLABORATION.md §3.3 forbids. It is deliberately **not** `@unique` — phone is free-form text here (three different validation rules exist in the repo, and two live rows already differ only by internal spacing), so a unique index would enforce nothing while looking like it does.

**Spending a verified pair is a DELETE of both rows inside the register transaction**, asserting `count === 2`. That is a compare-and-swap: a concurrent second register deletes 0 and loses. It also destroys the plaintext code at the moment it stops being needed, and leaves the table holding only in-flight signups — which is exactly what the console should show. The first cut of the model conflated "code entered correctly" with "spent" in a single `consumedAt`; that would have let a verified **phone** challenge be replayed across several registrations (email is accidentally protected by its unique constraint, phone is not), hence the corrective rename.

**API**: `POST /auth/recruiter/otp/request` (202 for every well-formed destination, so it is not an enumeration oracle) and `/verify` (timing-safe compare; the attempt gate is a conditional `updateMany` claim, not check-then-act). Register asserts both channels verified, unexpired and bound to the exact submitted address. `RegisterRecruiterDto` gains `phone` + `signupId` and deliberately **no** verification boolean — verified by test that forged `emailVerified: true` in the body is ignored. `POST /admin/otp-sessions/:id/reveal` is `AdminGuard`'d and writes an `OTP_CODE_REVEALED` audit row **with the code excluded from the diff**. Hourly purge added to the existing job-lifecycle queue (its daily 02:00 sweep is far too slow for a plaintext credential). **Wires `killswitch.new_registrations`, which existed as a seeded, critical-classified key with zero enforcement call sites anywhere** — flipping it previously did nothing.

**Rate limiting**: the per-challenge 5-attempt cap alone was decorative, because omitting `signupId` mints a fresh row with a fresh budget for any destination. Added a Postgres-only cap of 3 live challenges per `(channel, destination)`, taken under two transaction-scoped advisory locks. Postgres rather than the existing Redis guard specifically because that guard **fails open**, which would defeat the control.

**Recruiter form**: a `VerifiableField` island per channel, each owning its own `useId()`s, state machine, OTP sub-field and live regions. Editing a verified value clears the tick, and a response landing after an edit is discarded. Submit stays **enabled** with a standing explanation (withdrawn once both are verified, so it never asserts an unmet requirement beside two ticks).

**sadmin**: new "OTP Sessions" item and page — one row per signup attempt, six columns, newest first, paginated over distinct `signupId`. The list query **never selects `code`**: it is a server component, so selecting it would ship every live code in the RSC payload. Digits appear only behind a per-row audited Reveal, and never for an expired, verified or burnt challenge.

**Review**: adversarial 5-lens pass, 22 raised → 10 verified by two independent refuters each → **8 confirmed (4 distinct defects), all fixed**; 2 refuted; **12 were left unverified when verification was capped at 10, and 9 of those were fixed anyway on assessment**. Headline confirmed defects: the signup copy promised "we'll email you a code" when nothing is ever sent (the single most damaging one — a registrant would wait forever); `maxLength={10}` on the mobile input truncated the extremely common `+91 98765 43210` paste *before* normalisation could strip the country code, and typing it character-by-character silently settled on a **wrong 10-digit number that passed validation**; the client-chosen `signupId` guess-budget reset; and a stale destination sent through the signupId mint-gate.

**Two runtime-only bugs that no test could catch, found by actually running it**: (1) the advisory locks were taken with `$queryRaw`, and `pg_advisory_xact_lock()` returns `void`, which Prisma cannot deserialize — **every OTP request 500'd against a real Postgres while the suite stayed green**, because a mocked client returns `undefined` for anything. Fixed to `$executeRaw`, with a structural regression guard. (2) `register` 500'd on `User_pkey` — see the follow-up chip; that one is pre-existing and not caused by this branch.

**Verified end to end against a running stack**: the full API flow (binding enforced, replay rejected, forged verified-booleans rejected, rows deleted on spend), a complete browser signup landing signed-in on `/dashboard` with 0 console errors, `+91 98765 43210` normalising correctly in the live field, all five sadmin cell states rendering, a real Reveal writing a clean audit row, and server logs carrying only ids and channels. Advisory locks probed against live Postgres for mutual exclusion, non-blocking of distinct keys, and release on rollback. **Dev DB restored byte-for-byte afterwards** (31 users, 0 challenge rows). Gate green: typecheck 12/12 · tests 7/7 (API 683 → **791**, recruiter 53 → **83**, sadmin 131 → **175**, web 216) · build 5/5.

**Deliberate decisions worth knowing.** Registration **no longer sends the work-email verification link** and sets `User.emailVerified` / `Recruiter.workEmailVerified` true at creation: the OTP just proved control of that same address, so the link was redundant and new recruiters clear the post-job gate immediately. Codes are stored in **plaintext**, which knowingly departs from a rule this schema states three times — forced by the staff-relay design, with a short TTL, delete-on-spend, the hourly purge and the audited reveal as compensating controls. Two design-level risks were explicitly **accepted** by the owner rather than engineered away: a staff member can self-serve a code for a contact they do not own and register as that company (the slug then becomes permanently taken), and relay staff necessarily hold full `ADMIN` because `UserRole` has no tier below it — a `SUPPORT` role was considered and deferred. ⚠️ **This paragraph is the authoritative decision record**: CLAUDE.md §10 puts ADRs in `docs/adr/`, but `/docs/` is gitignored, so an ADR there would never reach the other two developers. A local copy sits at `docs/adr/0002-plaintext-signup-otp.md`; **`docs/` being both the prescribed ADR home and untracked is a process gap worth resolving.**

### `feature/recruiter-auth-ui-revamp` — recruiter sign-in / sign-up: two-pane split with a brand illustration · 2026-07-29

**Owner brief:** *"In recruiter portal, do a UI revamp of the sign in/log in or sign up page because currently they look basic. you can do something like illustrations on the left side, and the form on the right side. Ensure that you only work on the UI and not disturb any functionalities."* CLI merge to `develop` (`--no-ff`). SRS §4.9.

**What was there.** All four public routes shared one `(auth)` layout: a thin bordered header with the logo, then a `max-w-md` column centred in the viewport. Both forms were `'use client'` pages rendering an h1 and a `space-y-4` stack of atoms. That is the whole reason they read as basic — a 448px column on an empty page, using none of the brand-navy/cyan system the authed shell received in `feature/recruiter-brand-theme`.

**The panel.** Left pane is the *same fixed brand navy as the authed sidebar rail* (`--color-primary-600`), so signing in previews the shell you land in. Two details that follow from that surface are load-bearing rather than decorative: the logo must pass `onDark`, because the auto path keys off `[data-theme="dark"]` and this app mounts no `ThemeProvider`, so it would have rendered the navy logo on navy; and the focus ring must be overridden to white, because the inherited `--color-ring` measures **1.96:1** on this navy, under the 3:1 WCAG 1.4.11 floor (**15.37:1** after). Below `lg` the panel is dropped rather than stacked — stacking it would open the sign-in page on artwork with the email field off-screen — and a compact navy strip keeps the brand and the home link the old layout carried.

**The illustrations.** CLAUDE.md §2 bans cute illustrations and mascots, so "illustration" here means the house style `ArticleCover` already established: flat geometry in navy/cyan/alpha-white, built from the product's own primitives — a job card, a trend panel and an applicant list with one row shortlisted for sign-in; a compose card with three fields and Publish beside Save-as-draft, next to a list of reached candidates, for sign-up. Both are `aria-hidden` and every fact they depict is stated in real text beside them. The literal `white` fills are deliberate: this panel's background never follows the light/dark token swap, and alpha-white is the same system the navy rail already uses.

**The structural decision, and the bug that forced it.** The panel first lived in the `(auth)` layout and resolved its copy from the `x-canonical-pathname` header the middleware already sets — no client JS, no `usePathname`, and the two inherited routes needed no edit. It was broken. The App Router does not re-render a shared layout when navigating between two of its own children, and the `/login` ↔ `/register` links are the only in-product path between those pages, so clicking "Create one" left *"Welcome back to your hiring desk."* and the sign-in illustration beside the sign-up form until a hard reload. The shell now lives in each page — the segment that actually re-renders — with the two forms extracted verbatim into `LoginForm` / `RegisterForm` client islands so the pages are server components and no presentational code enters the client bundle. The `(auth)` layout is deleted; `verify-email` and `accept-invite` wrap themselves in the same shell with brand-level copy. A side effect worth recording: the header-reading version had forced all four routes dynamic, and the per-page version puts `/login` and `/register` back to **`○ Static`**, matching develop's route table exactly.

**Functional contract held.** Same endpoints (`POST /auth/login`, `POST /auth/recruiter/register`), same bodies, same `credentials: 'include'`, same `safeNext` → `router.push(next)` → `router.refresh()`, same `router.push('/dashboard')`, same field names, `autoComplete`, `required`, `maxLength`, and the same `role="alert"` live region. Accessibility improved in passing: helper text is bound with `aria-describedby`, ids come from `useId()`, `fg-subtle` hints moved to `fg-muted`, the error text came off raw `--color-danger` (**4.0:1**, a pre-existing AA failure) onto the repo's theme-aware `color-mix` recipe (**6.43:1**), and the submit button is a 44px target. A show/hide password toggle was added at the owner's choice, mirroring `apps/sadmin`'s.

**Adversarial 5-lens review, 3 independent skeptics per finding (refute-by-default): 34 raised → 5 confirmed across 3 distinct defects, 29 refuted.** Two of the five are the *same* defect surfaced by a second lens — the soft-navigation bug and the static/dynamic regression were each raised twice. (The skeptic vote is stochastic: a cached re-run of the same review scored the second copy of the static/dynamic finding 4 → 5. Recorded at the higher count; no additional defect exists, and every one of the three distinct defects was fixed and re-verified before merge.)  (1) **HIGH** the stale panel on soft navigation — raised independently by two lenses and reproduced in both directions with a window canary proving a soft nav. (2) **MED** the `headers()` call forced all four routes dynamic behind a comment wrong on both of its claims: `register/page.tsx` never read search params at all, and login's `useSearchParams` is Suspense-wrapped, which is precisely what *keeps* a route prerenderable. (3) **MED** the dashed "reach" curve in the sign-up illustration started inside the button gutter and ran through the Save-as-draft pill — 11 of 21 sampled points inside it — reading as a strike-through; removed rather than rerouted, because the two cards are 12 units apart and any connector there is decoration. (4) **MED** the decorative aside forced the page to scroll on a real 1366×768 laptop (~650px viewport after browser chrome; measured **24px over**) — the vertical rhythm now clamps against viewport height, so only real form content can ever scroll.

**Gate green** (typecheck **12/12** · tests **7/7** — recruiter **32 → 53** · build **5/5**). **Browser-verified against the running stack**: sign-in → `/dashboard`; wrong password → the alert with submit re-enabled and still on `/login`; `?next=%2Fjobs%3Fstatus%3DACTIVE` → lands on `/jobs?status=ACTIVE`; register duplicate-email → "Email already registered" with all four values preserved; the panel swap proved both directions post-fix; **0 contrast failures on both pages** (min 5.21, canvas-composited and sanity-checked at 21:1 black-on-white); no horizontal overflow at 1440/1366/1280/1024/1023/768/375; 0 console errors on a clean tab.

**Not verified, deliberately:** the register *happy* path. A real `RESEND_API_KEY` is configured locally, so creating an account would write `User`+`Recruiter`+`Company` rows (polluting the 14-company set a teammate's Employer Management page is verified against) and attempt an outbound email to a bogus address. The submit handler is byte-identical to develop's, the duplicate-email path proves the request/response wiring, and login's happy path proves `push` + `refresh` after a cookie-setting response.

**Follow-up (pre-existing, not touched):** the footer link now uses `--color-primary-700`, matching the recruiter app's established link colour but not theme-following; if this app ever mounts a `ThemeProvider`, that link and several sibling uses across the portal need a `dark:` pass together.

**Local environment note:** midway through this branch the pnpm store lost files under `@prisma/client` and `argon2`, which 500'd the dev server and failed `pnpm build` with `Module not found: '@prisma/client/runtime/client'`. Repaired with `rm -rf node_modules **/node_modules` + `pnpm store prune` + `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` + `pnpm db:generate`. Unrelated to this branch; recorded because the symptom looks like a code fault.

### `feature/sadmin-employer-management` — Employer Management: master list + employer profile · 2026-07-29

**The Super Admin portal's third menu item.** CLI merge to `develop` (`--no-ff`). SRS §4.9 / §4.16. Owner brief: *"a new dashboard menu item called Employer Management … recruiter master list — all registered employers with columns for company name, contact person, email, phone, registration date, account status. Actions: View Profile."*

**One row per Company, decided before any code.** "Recruiter master list" and "all registered employers" pull in opposite directions, and the schema makes them genuinely different products: 14 companies against 10 recruiters, with four companies having none. The owner chose the company as the row key, which makes four of the six requested columns *derived* — only `Company.name` and `Company.createdAt` sit on the row itself.

**The derivation is the feature.** `resolveContact()` takes the active OWNER; failing that, the longest-standing active recruiter of a lesser role, flagged as a stand-in. A **deactivated recruiter is never offered as a contact** — deactivation revokes sessions and blocks re-authentication, so they cannot be reached through the platform, and offering them would be a phone number that goes nowhere. The role is rendered beside every name so a Member standing in for a removed owner cannot read as the owner. Precedence is an explicit `Record<RecruiterRole, number>`, not `orderBy: { companyRole: 'asc' }` — that ordering appears to work only because the enum happens to be declared OWNER, ADMIN, MEMBER, and reordering it later would silently invert the console's idea of who speaks for a company. Ties break on join date then id, because **nothing in the database prevents a second OWNER** (the last-owner rule lives in the recruiter service).

**Account status carries both axes, and the third state earns its place.** Active / Deactivated / **No account holder**, with verification as a secondary mark. `DEACTIVATED` and `NO_ACCOUNT` are identical in the data — no active recruiter — and opposite in meaning: an employer that left, versus a company nobody ever registered for. Four seeded companies are the latter, and they hold **live jobs with nobody managing them**; the detail page says so in words rather than leaving three em-dashes to be read as an ordinary row.

**Three data facts that changed the design, all measured against the database rather than assumed.** Registration date is **`Company.createdAt`**: self-registration into an existing company is rejected by the API (*"ask an admin on that team to invite you"*), so every recruiter after the first joined an already-registered employer and their `createdAt` means "this person joined". Verification reads `kyc?.status ?? 'NOT_SUBMITTED'` because **13 of 14 companies have no `CompanyKyc` row at all** and the schema states the row's absence *is* that state. And the phone column is **12-of-14 empty** — kept, on the owner's explicit call, because it is a real field a recruiter can fill and "not provided" is actionable; deliberately **not** falling back to `altPocPhone`, which is a *backup* contact and would print one person's number under another's name.

**Read path.** Postgres directly in the RSC, with **no new API module** — a deliberate reversal of this branch's own opening WORKLOG claim. `lib/admin-api.ts` states the API path exists for logic that must agree with a *mutation* endpoint; this surface is display-only and has none, so it follows the dashboard's precedent and the repo's reads/writes split. That removed six files and an `app.module.ts` edit, and left `apps/api` byte-untouched. Job counts come from a single `groupBy` with **every lifecycle bucket named**, so no figure can quietly fold in DRAFT — the 53-vs-52 defect this repo shipped once already. GSTIN, PAN and the registration number are **not selected at all**: PII under the DPDP Act, and `admin-kyc` masks them even in its own list.

**A `loading.tsx` was written, measured, and deleted.** Adding one to the `employers` segment opened a Suspense boundary that flushes the shell before `notFound()` and `redirect()` throw, so the response had already committed 200 and both **degraded silently — 404 → 200 and 307 → 200**. Because `loading.tsx` also wraps nested segments, the single file broke the list *and* `[id]`. Proven in both directions on a running server. This also reclassifies an open follow-up from the job-moderation PR: `/jobs` having no `loading.tsx` is a **constraint, not an oversight**, and both call sites now carry a comment saying so.

**Adversarial 5-lens review** (data correctness · truthfulness · a11y/semantics · isolation/security · tests/conventions), each finding put to **three independent skeptics** and kept only on a majority: **30 raised → 5 confirmed, 25 refuted.** **(a)** An id above int32 answered **500, not 404** — `Company.id` is a Postgres `integer`, so `/employers/2147483648` threw out of Prisma instead of returning null, and with no `try`/`catch` and no segment `error.tsx` it escaped to `global-error`. This is the **first sadmin detail route reading Prisma directly**, so `adminApiGet`'s error handling was not there to absorb it; `apps/recruiter`'s job pages already carry the exact bound with the same comment. `Number()` also accepts hex and exponent, so `/employers/0x1a` and `/employers/1e1` rendered real companies under non-canonical URLs. **(b)** The **Deactivated / Removed labels failed WCAG AA** — raw `--color-danger` is `#e62b34`, computing **4.41:1** on the card and **4.02:1** once the row hovers to `bg-muted`, making the one label an admin most needs to read the least legible thing on the page; fixed with the theme-aware `color-mix` `apps/recruiter` already uses (**7.03:1 / 6.45:1**), with the compiled stylesheet checked to confirm Tailwind actually emits the `color:` declaration. **(c+d)** Two comments justified the id tiebreaks with a seed behaviour **that does not exist** — `demo.ts` has no `$transaction`, and all 14 companies and all 10 recruiters have distinct `createdAt` values; the tiebreaks stay (offset pagination is only sound on a total order) but the invented evidence is gone. **(e)** The team table's `overflow-x-auto` had **no focusable descendants and no tabindex**, so once it scrolled the hidden columns were Joined and Status — the two that explain a Deactivated employer.

**Gate (integrated):** typecheck **12/12** · tests **7/7** (API 683 / web 216 / recruiter 32 / **sadmin 86 → 131** / auth 17 / feature-flags 37 / observability 19) · build **5/5**, both routes correctly dynamic (`ƒ`).

**Verified against the running stack and cross-checked against SQL.** All 14 rows matched the database value-for-value: the ten resolved owners, the four "No account holder" rows, and exactly the two real phone numbers. Detail pages checked for three contrasting employers — Acme (**Live 3 / Drafts 1 / All postings 4**, proving DRAFT stays out of the posted figures; KYC pending with legal name and signatory; 1 support ticket), Margdarshi (no recruiter, *"It still has 3 live jobs on the platform"*, 18 applications) and Nimbus (7 live, 105 applications). **Three code paths unreachable in the seed were forced and then restored**: an owner demoted to MEMBER rendered **"Member"**; a fully removed team rendered **"Deactivated"** with the contact blanked while the team table still showed who was removed and when; and eight temporary companies proved pagination — **20 + 2 rows, 22 unique ids, zero overlap across the seam**, with `?page=99` redirecting **307 → `?page=2`**, the real last page. Bad ids (`999`, `abc`, `0`, `-1`, `1.5`, `0x1a`, `1e1`, `2147483648`, `1e20`) all **404**; anonymous access **307 → `/sadmin/login?next=%2Femployers`**. **Database restored byte-exact** afterwards (14 / 10 / 10 / 10 / 1 / 54 / 371, with `updatedAt` on the mutated row untouched).

**Deferred / follow-ups.** (1) **No search and no sortable columns** — 14 rows fit one page today, but this is the surface that most obviously needs both, and sorting by contact person is **not a small change**: Prisma cannot order a parent by a to-many relation's field, so it needs raw SQL. (2) `Company` has **no index on `name` or `createdAt`** (only `slug` unique, `industryId`, `headquartersCityId`), so the default ordering is a sort of the whole table — invisible at 14 rows, worth an index before it is thousands. (3) The list is **read-only**: no suspend, no verify, no reactivate. The moment any of those lands it must move to an `apps/api` module so `AdminGuard`, Zod and audit logging apply — and "account status" would then need a real `Company` column, the schema lock and a named migration. (4) `pnpm db:seed` alone creates **zero companies**, so a fresh clone sees the empty state until the demo seed runs. (5) `lib/employers/queries.ts` has **no unit tests** — the pure logic was deliberately extracted into `format.ts` where vitest can reach it, but the Prisma reads themselves are covered only by the browser verification above.

### `feature/navbar-mega-menu-console` — Navbar mega-menu redesigned as "The Console" · 2026-07-29

**Seeker navbar mega-menu redesign** (SRS §4.1 / §4.7). CLI merge to `develop` (`--no-ff`). **`apps/web` only** — `packages/*`, the other apps, `theme.css` and the icon barrel are byte-untouched; **no schema/migration/feature-flag/API/shared-surface-lock**. The only edits outside the self-contained `components/nav/` folder are one app-owned keyframe appended to `globals.css` and two added test cases.

**Why:** the owner rejected the previously-shipped **"Native tiles"** mega-menu (and, before it, two editorial-column attempts at the career-advice hub) and asked for "pure modern, unique and professional… senior frontend engineer experience". **Process:** a judge-panel design Workflow generated **four fresh silhouettes** (master-detail navigator / bento metric board / spotlight marquee / refined rich list), three judges scored them on distinctiveness · premium craft · brand-flat fit · a11y · data-honesty · buildability, and a design lead merged the two consensus winners (they were the same silhouette) and shortlisted **three genuinely non-overlapping directions**. Those were pitched as **one interactive full-fidelity Artifact** — real brand tokens, the real seed data, all three switchable — and the owner picked **A · The Console**. (This artifact-before-code loop is the same one that unblocked the career-advice redesign; the two prior rejections both came from designs judged blind.)

**What shipped.** A two-pane master/detail popover replacing the tile grid. A **216px facet rail** — a real ARIA `tablist` with roving tabindex, Arrow/Home/End, and hover === focus === select — sits beside a **detail pane**, and the two are stitched by **ONE 3px cyan indicator that glides** between facets (the "one thing moving" discipline that reads senior in Stripe/Linear, instead of N hover states blinking). Beneath both runs a full-width footer: the live count, the two honest quick-views, and the panel's single filled element (a navy CTA with a cyan arrow). **Three craft moves do the work:** (1) the detail holds a fixed **`min-h-[15rem]`** so switching facets **never resizes the floating popover** — browser-measured at a constant 298px across all four Jobs facets; (2) every Jobs row carries an **honest proportion bar** — fill = that row's share of its own facet's maximum, with an 8% floor so a count of 1 stays visible and the leader capped in cyan — and it is **deliberately never animated**, because animating a data length would misrepresent it; (3) one elevation and a strict cyan-as-marks-only budget (indicator, bar cap, hover chevrons, the featured top hairline, the CTA arrow — **no cyan text anywhere**). **Jobs** = Roles / Locations / Industries / Skills, each facet dropped below two items so the rail always reads intentional. **Companies** = Collections / Industries / Featured, where industry rows are deliberately **count-free** (the only number held for an industry is a JOB count, which beside a companies collection would misread as a company count). New client island **`FacetTabs.tsx`** receives the rail icons, every detail pane and the footer as **server-rendered ReactNode slots** (the slot pattern taken one level deeper), so the presentational subtree — rows, `CompanyLogo`, the url builders — never enters the client bundle while all links stay in the crawlable HTML; inactive panes carry the `hidden` attribute, which natively removes them from the tab order and the a11y tree. Retired `NavTile.tsx` + `menu-chrome.tsx`; **`PrimaryNav`'s open/close contract, `lib/nav/menu-data.ts` and `nav-hrefs.ts` are unchanged**, so the cached loader, canonical URLs, pointer-intent, single-open, Escape, outside-click and inert-when-closed all carry over untouched.

**Adversarial 4-lens review** (correctness-RSC · a11y · brand-craft · isolation-perf, every finding independently refuted): **19 raised → 13 confirmed (8 distinct), all fixed.** Two were real interaction defects introduced by the new rail: **(MED)** clicking a facet let the browser focus the `<button>`, which satisfied `PrimaryNav`'s "keep the panel open while focus is inside the group" guard — written for keyboard users — and so **defeated the mouse-leave auto-close, pinning an 834px panel to the sticky header until Escape**; the tab now `preventDefault`s mousedown so only keyboard selection ever moves focus. **(MED)** a stray pointer sweeping the rail while keyboard focus sat on a link in the open pane **hid the focused element, dropping focus to `<body>` and closing the entire popover**; pointer-driven selection now bails while focus is inside the pane, leaving click and keyboard selection untouched. Also: **(LOW)** the indicator offset was a hardcoded **38px** driving rows sized in `rem`, so it drifted onto the wrong row for anyone whose browser font size isn't 16px → now `calc(index * 2.375rem)`, the same unit as `h-9 + gap-0.5`; **(LOW)** `active` was never reconciled with the data-derived tab set, so a shrinking set would drop the rail out of the tab order and blank the pane → clamped at read time; **(LOW)** **"Top rated" and "All companies" were adjacent rows resolving to the identical `/companies` URL** (rating *is* the directory's default sort) → "All companies" now points at `?sort=name` and is labelled A–Z, locked by **+2 regression tests** including one asserting all four Collections destinations are distinct; **(LOW)** the pane reused `.rise`, a 250ms `translateY(8px)` *entrance* keyframe, so travelling down the rail shifted the pane and blanked it two or three times → replaced with a dedicated **opacity-only `pane-swap`** (0.35 → 1 at `--duration-fast`), so a pass-through shimmers rather than flashing and nothing moves while the eye tracks the indicator; **(LOW)** the zero-facet fallback was too narrow for the footer it held → reduced to count + CTA, which is also the honest content when no facet has data; **(NIT)** a `max-w-[calc(100vw-1.5rem)]` guard that could not bound the right edge (the panel is positioned from its trigger, not the viewport) → dropped rather than left as decoration, plus an unused `EmployerItem.slug` removed.

**Gate (integrated, `develop` @ `07a63bd`):** typecheck **12/12** · tests **7/7** (API 683 / web **216** / recruiter 32 / sadmin 86 / auth 17 / feature-flags 37 / observability 19) · build **5/5** apps. **Browser-verified** (real seed data, via JS probes — preview screenshots time out on these pages): the indicator lands at exactly 0/38/76/114px and matches the measured row offsets; panel height stays 298px across every facet; hover-select, `ArrowDown`, `End` and roving tabindex all behave; both MED fixes re-verified after the fix (a cancelable mousedown reports `defaultPrevented`, and a stray hover holds the pane with focus intact while keyboard selection still switches); proportion bars render true relative widths (Bangalore 17 → 100%, Pune 6 → 35%, Mumbai 5 → 29%); the four Collections URLs are distinct; **113 text nodes measured with ZERO contrast failures** (labels 17.92:1, active tab 15.56:1, footer figures 15.37:1, muted 7.79:1, CTA white-on-navy 15.37:1); **no horizontal overflow at 1360 or the tight 1024** breakpoint; closed panels keep 27 links in the DOM but `inert`; focus-open, single-open swap, outside-pointerdown and Escape all still close; 0 console errors. **Note for teammates:** this branch was cut after the job-moderation merges, so the panel's live count reads ~44 open roles rather than 52 — moderation is ON and some jobs now sit in review.

### `feature/job-moderation-queue-ui` — The /sadmin review queue, and moderation ON (PR 2 of 3) · 2026-07-29

**The surface that makes the flag safe to turn on, and the flip itself.** CLI merge to `develop` (`--no-ff`). SRS §4.9 / §7, CLAUDE.md §4. PR 1 shipped the backend with the flag still OFF because there was nothing to work the queue with; this adds it.

**The console.** `apps/sadmin/app/(authed)/jobs` — a queue and a review screen. The queue is FIFO on `submittedForReviewAt`, shows who posted what and how long it has waited, and states plainly when moderation is off so an empty list can't be misread as "all caught up". The review screen puts the whole posting on one page with the employer's **verification status** beside it — the single most useful signal a moderator has — plus Approve / Send back. Recruiter-authored text is rendered as **plain text, never markup**: this is untrusted content shown to staff for the express purpose of judging it, so it must not be able to inject anything into the console reviewing it.

**Two views, not a status filter.** Filtering the list on `JobStatus` was the obvious design and it is wrong: `ACTIVE` is every live job on the platform, the overwhelming majority never moderated, so an "approved" tab would have listed 52 jobs nobody looked at. The endpoint now takes `view=pending|decided`, where `decided` is `reviewedAt IS NOT NULL` — a human actually ruled on it. That view doubles as the **only readable record of a moderation decision anywhere in the product**: decisions write `ProfileAuditLog` rows, and that table has no read surface.

**First mutation surface in this app.** `lib/admin-api.ts` forwards the HttpOnly `access_token` cookie to the AdminGuard'd endpoints. `apps/sadmin` now has two data paths on purpose: the dashboard reads Postgres directly (plain display counts, no server logic worth centralising), while the queue goes through the API because the list and detail already exist as tested service methods and re-querying here would fork logic that has to agree with what the decision endpoint enforces. It is also the shape the `/admin` console migration arrives in.

**Turning the flag on took two mechanisms.** `seedFlags` upserts with `update: {}` — it deliberately never overwrites an admin-toggled flag — so flipping the seed default reaches **fresh databases only**. Every existing developer machine and deployed environment needed the companion migration `enable_job_moderation_flag`, guarded on `enabled = false` so it is a no-op on a fresh seed.

**Recruiter-facing, now that `PENDING_MODERATION` is routine rather than theoretical.** The badge reads **"Under review"** — something happening to the job, not something the recruiter left undone. Publishing used to redirect to `/jobs` in silence, which was survivable when a published job was instantly live and reads as failure when it is not; the wizard now carries the outcome through `?submitted=`, taken from **the API's returned status** rather than from what the wizard asked for, so it cannot claim a job went live when it went to review. Jobs-by-status always shows the review row, so a recruiter whose queue just cleared still sees that review is part of publishing. The edit page no longer says "changes to a live job go public immediately" while sitting under an "Under review" badge. And the demo seed stops re-asserting `status` on every run, which had silently emptied the review queue of anything a developer parked there to test.

**Adversarial 5-lens review** (flag-flip consequences · sadmin UI + basePath · API/data · recruiter surfaces · truthfulness/isolation; every finding independently refuted): **35 raised → 10 confirmed (7 distinct), all fixed; 25 refuted.** Two were copy the flag flip made false, both the same class the PR had already fixed elsewhere and simply missed: the **Reopen confirm dialog** still promised the job "goes live again immediately — it reappears in search and starts accepting applications", when `reopen()` now routes it to review (unindexed, applications 403'd, and `close()` 400s on `PENDING_MODERATION` so there is no self-service way back) — the `publish` entry three lines below carries a comment explaining it was reworded for exactly this reason; and three **FAQ answers** still described the pre-moderation model, including a status glossary titled "Draft, Active, Closed and Expired" that omitted the one state every new posting now sits in — the page a recruiter is steered to when their job does not appear. The sharpest was a formatter stating **the opposite of the truth**: the one-sided salary band collapsed min-only and max-only into the same `+` suffix, so a job capped at ₹12 LPA rendered **"₹12+ LPA" — a floor**, while the candidate-facing page rendered "Up to ₹12 LPA" for the same row. **The unit test had asserted the wrong string**, so the suite endorsed it — the identical false-premise pattern the previous PR's review caught, reproduced here. Also: `EMPLOYMENT_LABEL` invented `CONTRACT` and `TEMPORARY` (neither exists) while omitting the real `CONTRACTOR`, which fell through to the raw-enum fallback and rendered "CONTRACTOR" to the reviewer — both tables are now keyed by the Prisma enums themselves, so a missing or invented member is a **compile error** rather than something a test must remember; an over-range `?page` rendered "No jobs are waiting for review" with no count, table or pagination, factually wrong and a dead end (now redirects to the real last page, sharing its href builder with the pagination links); and three stale comments, including one crediting the migration's `enabled = false` guard with preventing an admin's later toggle being clobbered — Prisma records each migration and skips applied ones, so it runs at most once and cannot see a later value.

**Gate (integrated):** typecheck **12/12** · tests **7/7** (API **680 → 683** / web 214 / recruiter 32 / **sadmin 60 → 86** / auth 17 / feature-flags 37 / observability 19) · build **5/5**.

**Verified end to end in a real browser, against a real posting.** A job posted through the API as the demo recruiter landed in `PENDING_MODERATION`; anonymous visitors got **404**, it was **absent from Elasticsearch**, and its owner still got 200. The sadmin queue showed it with "Waiting: Today" and no flag-off banner. Send back with no reason was blocked client-side; with a reason it flipped to DRAFT with the text stored, the decision form correctly disappeared, and a notification and audit row were written. "Recently decided" showed **1** job — not all 52 live ones. The recruiter republished (stale reason and review fields cleared, quota flag back to true), and Approve in the UI produced ACTIVE with `postedAt` refreshed and the job **now present in Elasticsearch (200) and publicly visible (200)** — the side effects genuinely replayed. The two review fixes were re-verified in the live page: the over-range redirect returns `307 → /sadmin/jobs`, and the detail page renders **"Up to ₹12 LPA"** and **"Contract"**. **Database and Elasticsearch restored byte-exact** (52 ACTIVE + 1 DRAFT, 0 stray notifications/audit rows/quota keys).

**Deferred / follow-ups.** (1) **PR 3 (`feature/sadmin-admin-migration`)** — moving feature-flags · audit-log · kyc-review · support out of `apps/web/app/admin`. It also carries the moderation flag's **criticality** (a confirm modal + mandatory audit reason), which matters more now the flag is ON in both directions, plus the `"/admin/support"` string hardcoded in a live transactional email and the missing `NEXT_PUBLIC_SADMIN_URL`. (2) **A recruiter still cannot withdraw a job from review** — `close()` 400s on `PENDING_MODERATION`; they can edit or delete it. Worth a "Cancel review" action. (3) **No `PENDING_MODERATION` fixture in any seed**, so a fresh clone sees an empty queue until someone posts through the wizard. (4) The "usually within a day" line in the submission banner is a **service promise with nothing enforcing it** — no SLA, no alerting on queue age. (5) `apps/sadmin/app/(authed)/jobs` has **no `loading.tsx`**, unlike the dashboard.

### `feature/job-moderation-api` — Admin approve/reject for jobs awaiting review (PR 1 of 3) · 2026-07-29

**Closes the `moderation.jobs.enabled` trapdoor.** CLI merge to `develop` (`--no-ff`). SRS §4.9 / §7, CLAUDE.md §4. Owner brief: *"when a job is posted its status goes into under review or similar status so that this issue is resolved"*, plus migrating the old `/admin` console into `/sadmin` — split into three PRs, of which this is the backend.

**The problem.** `moderation.jobs.enabled` routes newly published jobs to `PENDING_MODERATION` instead of `ACTIVE`. It was seeded, toggleable from `/admin/feature-flags`, and read by exactly one service — but **nothing in the entire repo could move a job out of that status.** `close()` rejected it explicitly, `reopen()` required CLOSED/EXPIRED, `publish()` required DRAFT, `update()` never writes `status`, and the nightly expiry sweep selects `status: 'ACTIVE'`. The only exit was `DELETE`. Flipping the flag on therefore stranded every job published thereafter, permanently, and the sadmin dashboard had shipped a count for a queue with no surface behind it.

**Schema** (2 migrations, schema lock held). `add_job_moderation`: `Job.submittedForReviewAt` / `reviewedAt` / `reviewedById` / `rejectionReason`, plus `NotificationType` and `ProfileAuditAction` values `JOB_APPROVED` / `JOB_REJECTED` (one per outcome, mirroring the `KYC_VERIFIED`/`KYC_REJECTED` split). `add_job_review_queue_index_and_quota_flag`: `Job.postQuotaConsumed` + `@@index([status, submittedForReviewAt])`. **No new `JobStatus` value** — rejection returns the job to DRAFT with a reason, which keeps the five hand-maintained copies of the status set (Prisma enum, `JobStatusBadge`, `VALID_STATUSES`, the API Zod enum, the sadmin trends SQL) untouched and reuses the existing resubmit path.

**API.** New `apps/api/src/admin-jobs` — `GET /admin/jobs` (queue, defaults to pending), `GET /admin/jobs/:id` (the whole posting plus resolved skill/city names and the company's KYC status, since "is this employer verified" is the reviewer's most useful signal), `PATCH /admin/jobs/:id`. `AdminGuard`, `.strict()` Zod DTOs, reason required on reject. **Deliberately not gated by `moderation.jobs.enabled`** — turning intake off must not strand a queue that already has jobs in it, the same rule `admin-kyc` and `admin-support` state. The decision is an **atomic `updateMany` guarded on `status`, committed in one transaction with its `ProfileAuditLog` row**, so two admins clicking Approve cannot both index the job and both notify; the loser gets a 409. (`AdminKycService.review()` does a bare read-then-update and has this race — deliberately not copied.)

**Approval is not a status write.** `JobPublishEffectsService` was extracted from `RecruiterJobsService` so approval replays the identical **ES index → alert fanout → cache purge → email** sequence; a bare `update({status:'ACTIVE'})` would leave a job live in Postgres but absent from search and alerts. It also **re-clears a stale `expiresAt`**: a job can sit in review past its own expiry, where the ACTIVE-only sweep cannot see it, and would otherwise go live and be killed by the very next nightly sweep with the recruiter's quota already spent. The extraction follows the precedent of `missingPublishFields()`, pulled out for exactly this reason when `create(PUBLISH)` became a second make-live path.

**Two real holes this work exposed, both fixed here.** (1) **`reopen()` was a moderation bypass** — it set ACTIVE unconditionally, never read the flag, and fired the full side-effect trio plus the "your job is live" email, so Close → Reopen relisted without review. It now honours the flag, and re-reviews rather than trusting the earlier approval, because `update()` may freely rewrite a CLOSED job's title and description without triggering review — Close → Edit → Reopen would otherwise launder content no admin had seen. (2) **The public seeker job page served unpublished jobs.** `apps/web/app/job/[slug]/page.tsx` loaded the row with a bare `findUnique` and no status predicate, so a DRAFT or PENDING_MODERATION job rendered a full 200 with description, salary and an unconditional `JobPosting` JSON-LD block — and the canonical 308 ran *before* any status check, putting the job title in a `Location` header for anyone who guessed a numeric id. New `lib/job/visibility.ts` 404s non-public jobs for everyone except the poster, their collaborators and admins (so the recruiter ⋮ menu's "Preview" keeps working, and an admin can open the page they are being asked to approve); `generateMetadata` applies the identical rule, since it runs independently and would otherwise leak the title into `<title>`.

**Adversarial 5-lens review** (state-machine/concurrency · security/disclosure · isolation · test quality · comment truthfulness; each finding independently refuted): **33 raised → 7 confirmed (2 distinct), all fixed; 26 refuted.** Both confirmed defects were **introduced by this branch's own `reopen()` change**, which made reopen a third entry into `PENDING_MODERATION` without reconciling it with the other two. **(a) Free post slots** (found independently by four lenses): `moderate(REJECT)` refunded unconditionally on the premise that "the slot was consumed at submit time" — true for `create(PUBLISH)` and `publish()`, false for `reopen()`, which deliberately consumes nothing. Nothing on the row recorded which path produced the pending state, so every rejected relist handed back a slot that was never taken — farmable once per closed job. `Job.postQuotaConsumed` now records it and the refund is conditional. **(b) Reopened jobs jumped the queue**: the FIFO queue sorted on `postedAt`, which `reopen()` never refreshes, so a job reopened today carried its original go-live date and outranked everything genuinely waiting — and the schema comment asserted an invariant (`submittedForReviewAt` equals `postedAt` while pending) that this branch's own code broke. The queue now sorts on `submittedForReviewAt`, backed by the new index. The review also caught a test that **had baked in the same false premise as the code** (titled "REJECT refunds the post slot consumed at submit time", it could only ever confirm a refund happened, never that one was owed) — the exact pattern that let a HIGH-severity bug ship green on the previous sadmin PR.

**Smaller fixes carried in the same PR.** `reindex.ts` had no status filter, so a full Elasticsearch rebuild wrote DRAFT and PENDING_MODERATION documents into the live alias — invisible only because `searchJobs()` happens to default to ACTIVE, which made an unapproved job's privacy depend on a default in an unrelated module; it now indexes ACTIVE only, converging with what the incremental `syncJob` path maintains. `quota.refund()` DECR'd unconditionally, and Redis DECR on a *missing* key creates it at −1 with no TTL — near-unreachable while refunds only fired on a lost publish race, but moderation makes them routine, so the very first rejection of a job submitted on an earlier day would have hit it. The apply guard interpolated the raw Postgres enum into user-facing copy (*"This job is pending_moderation and no longer accepts applications"* — and "no longer" is wrong for a job that was never live). `ClosedJobNotice` filled with `--color-surface-subtle`, a token not defined in `theme.css`, so the banner rendered with no background — the last unfixed instance of one `PostJobFlow` and `PostJobWizard` already record removing, and precisely the banner a pending job shows. `cache-purge`'s `PATH_MAP` gained `'moderation.jobs.enabled': []` so toggling it stops needlessly purging the seeker homepage.

**Gate (integrated, `develop` @ `30d62f1` — a teammate merged `feature/navbar-mega-menu` mid-flight and it was integrated first):** typecheck **12/12** · tests **7/7** (API **637 → 680** / web **199 → 214** / recruiter 32 / sadmin 60 / auth 17 / feature-flags 37 / observability 19) · build **5/5**.

**Verified against a running stack, not just unit tests.** Anon on a pending job → **404**; ACTIVE control → 200. A guessed id with a wrong slug on a pending job → **404 with no `Location` header** (the title leak closed) while the same trick on an ACTIVE job still 308s correctly. Owner preview → 200. Recruiter cookie on `/admin/jobs` → **403**, anon → **401**, reject with no reason → **400** naming `reason`. Reject → DRAFT with the reason stored, `postedAt` **not** moved, notification + audit row written, `postedAt` untouched. Approve with a deliberately stale `expiresAt` of 2020-01-01 → ACTIVE, `postedAt` refreshed, **expiry cleared**, ES doc present, notification sent. The quota fix was proved in **both** directions: reopen → reject leaves the counters at **3/3** (the leak would have shown 2/2), while publish → reject still refunds **4 → 3**. Queue ordering proved with a constructed case — a job submitted two days ago ranks ahead of one reopened today carrying a January `postedAt` — and the new index confirmed usable via a forced `EXPLAIN` (the planner correctly prefers a seq scan at 53 rows). Full reindex: **52 indexed**, the DRAFT job **404 in ES**, 0 non-ACTIVE docs. **Database and Elasticsearch restored byte-exact afterwards** (52 ACTIVE + 1 DRAFT, original timestamps, flag back OFF, 0 stray notifications/audit rows/quota keys, ES back in parity at 52).

**Two corrections to assumptions made during planning, both caught by measuring.** (1) The recon reported that a pending job's page sits in the shared edge cache, which would have defeated the visibility gate. It does not: the route reads cookies, so Next emits `Cache-Control: no-cache, must-revalidate`, which **overrides** the `public, s-maxage=60` that `next.config.ts` stamps on `/job/:slug`. The gate cannot be bypassed via caching — and, separately, this means the **SRS §4.2.10 edge TTL is not actually in effect for that route**, a pre-existing finding recorded below rather than fixed here. (2) A rejection reason containing an em-dash appeared to store as U+FFFD; that was Git Bash mangling the `curl` payload, not the application — a `—` JSON escape round-tripped as codepoint 8212 correctly.

**Deferred / follow-ups.** (1) **PR 2 (`feature/job-moderation-queue-ui`)** — the sadmin `/jobs` review queue + detail + approve/reject UI, the recruiter-facing "Under review" surfaces, and **flipping the flag ON**. Note the flip needs *two* mechanisms: `seedFlags` upserts with `update: {}` ("never overwrite an admin-toggled flag during reseed"), so a new seed default reaches **fresh databases only** and existing ones need a data migration. (2) **PR 3 (`feature/sadmin-admin-migration`)** — moving feature-flags · audit-log · kyc-review · support out of `apps/web/app/admin`. **Must land after PR 2**: `/admin/feature-flags` is the only surface that can turn moderation OFF, and a raw `UPDATE "FeatureFlag"` is not a substitute because `invalidateFlag()` is only called from `setFlag()`, so the 30s two-tier cache keeps serving the stale value. That PR also carries the moderation-flag criticality change (`keys.ts` + the hand-synced copy in `lib/admin/types.ts`, which it is already relocating) and the `"/admin/support"` string hardcoded in a live transactional email. (3) **A recruiter cannot withdraw a job from review** — `close()` still 400s on PENDING_MODERATION; they can edit or delete it. (4) **`quota.refund()` credits the current window**, so a Monday submission rejected on Wednesday decrements Wednesday's bucket. Raised three times in review and refuted each time as the only workable deferred refund — recorded here as a known limitation. (5) **No `PENDING_MODERATION` fixture in any seed**, and the demo seed sets `status: 'ACTIVE'` in both branches of its job upsert, so re-running it silently empties a queue an operator parked rows in for testing — worth fixing alongside PR 2. (6) **`apps/web/lib/cms/markdown.test.ts` is a flaky timeout under a full-suite run** — it failed once during this PR's gate and passed on a re-run and standalone. Nothing to do with this change (the file is untouched); it takes **~26s alone**, essentially all of it Shiki initialisation, so it trips the default timeout when the machine is loaded. Someone should either raise that file's `testTimeout` or warm Shiki once in a setup file.

### `feature/navbar-mega-menu` — Seeker navbar hover mega-menu for Jobs & Companies · 2026-07-29

**Navbar mega-menu** (SRS §4.1 / §4.7). CLI merge to `develop` (`--no-ff`). **`apps/web` only** — `packages/*` + other apps + `theme.css` byte-untouched; **no schema/migration/feature-flag/shared-surface-lock** (the new `components/nav/` folder + `lib/nav/` are self-contained; the only edits to existing files are `SiteHeader.tsx` and an additive `React cache()` wrap on `lib/home/queries.ts`). Owner ask: make the **Jobs** and **Companies** nav links open a smooth hover panel of job/company categories under headings, without looking like a copy of Naukri, while a plain click still navigates.

**Design process:** a judge-panel design Workflow (4 concepts → 3 judges → synthesis) picked "The Index" (an editorial contents-page), which the owner **rejected**; a quick AskUserQuestion then steered to the runner-up **"Native tiles"** — a mega-menu built from Career Queue's *own* homepage browse-grid tiles (a navy `primary-100` icon chip + label + live count + cyan hover chevron), so the menu reads as a native extension of the product rather than a generic dropdown. Each direction was pitched as a **full-fidelity interactive Artifact** (real brand tokens, real-shaped data) for approval before any code — the design-approval loop that has repeatedly de-risked these redesigns.

**What shipped.** `SiteHeader` (server) fetches a new **`loadNavMenuData()`** — a **date-free subset of `loadHomePageData` wrapped in `unstable_cache` (revalidate 1800)** so the header, rendered on every page, costs one shared computation per window and its counts can never disagree with the `/jobs` and `/companies` totals. It renders **`PrimaryNav`** (client island) and passes the two panels in as **server-rendered ReactNode props (slot pattern)**, so `JobsMegaPanel`/`CompaniesMegaPanel`/`NavTile`/`CompanyLogo`/the url builders never enter the client bundle. **Jobs panel:** a slim strip (live active-jobs count + honest quick-views *Newest this week* → `?postedWithin=7` / *Highest paying* → `?sort=salary_desc`) over four facet columns of tiles — By role (`/jobs?q=`), By location (`?city=`), By industry (`?industry=`), In-demand skills (`?skill=`), each with a real count; a group with < 2 items is dropped. **Companies panel:** strip (count + collections *Hiring now* `?hiring=1` / *Top rated* `/companies` / *Most reviewed* `?sort=reviews`) + an industry-tile column (`?category=` — **no count**, since the only number we hold is a job count that would misread as a company count) + a Featured Employers rail (top-rated companies via `CompanyLogo`, monochrome-navy star per owner call, `?…-overview-<id>`, the #1 keeping the cyan spotlight bar).
